convergence of Euclidean Gibbs states; Theorem~\ref{thm:MAIN}(1) is a Hamiltonian
counterpart, in norm on local von Neumann algebras, for the vacuum family. Problem 1 asks
for uniform local $L^p$ bounds on the Euclidean vacuum density. The note added on
\cite[p.~128]{GRS75} records Newman's solution of the small-coupling $p=1$ versions, so
the printed problems must not be read as an unqualified current open-problem claim. The
present proof neither establishes nor needs Problem 1: the identification step uses the
exact spectral identity of Proposition~\ref{prop:spectral}, and the convergence step uses
the Cauchy estimate.

\emph{Sharpness.} In the exactly solvable quadratic model the true rate is
$\mathrm{poly}(d)\,e^{-2m_1d}$ (companion note \texttt{m2/}: a round trip through the
region where the two Hamiltonians differ, at the local Agmon mass). $\Psi_{m_1}$ is
super-polynomial but sub-exponential. The present filter argument does not obtain the
sharp exponential rate; a proof of such a rate would require an additional resolvent or
Combes--Thomas input not supplied here.

\emph{Uniqueness.} $\omega_\infty$ is canonical for the cutoff family
(Corollary~\ref{cor:canonical}), and translation invariant without averaging. The theorem
does not identify every locally normal translation-invariant ground state of
$(\A,\alpha)$ with $\omega_\infty$. In particular, the Euclidean results \cite{AHZ,BDW}
do not by themselves provide the reverse reconstruction and state-space identification
needed for that Hamiltonian conclusion; companion note \texttt{m3/} records that missing
implication as its item (R3).

\emph{Beyond weak coupling.} Theorem~\ref{thm:dichotomy} is conditional on
Hypothesis~\ref{hyp:broken}. Under exactly those hypotheses, a proof based on a
cutoff-uniform gap cannot extend to that two-phase regime. The phase-transition results
\cite{GJS75,GJS76}, as cited here, are not used as a substitute for (B4)
(Remark~\ref{rem:gjs7576}), and this manuscript makes no unconditional assertion that its
Hamiltonian state space has the classification (B1)--(B4).

\emph{Part II.} Uniformity in a lattice/wavelet resolution --- the operator-algebraic
renormalization programme over the maps of \cite{MMST} --- is a separate problem with no
mathematical dependence in either direction, and its record lives in
\texttt{part\_ii/} and \texttt{strategy.md}. Its present state, in one paragraph: for the
free nearest-neighbour model, with lattice vacua $\omega^{(0)}_N$ and continuum vacuum
$\omega^{(0)}$, there are local Weyl unitaries with
$\liminf_{N\to\infty}\|\omega^{(0)}_N-\omega^{(0)}\circ\beta_N\|_{\mathfrak W_N(O)}
\ge e^{-\sqrt{2+\sqrt2}/4}-e^{-3\pi/16}>0$ for the Daubechies observable embedding
$\beta_N$, so no diagonal (finest-scale) local-state-norm error
$C\varepsilon_N^{\vartheta}$, $\vartheta>0$, is possible, and the meaningful comparison
is at fixed coarse scale; there, the free full sequence converges on each fixed coarse
Weyl generator with an explicit power rate, an interacting fixed-torus projective cluster
state exists unconditionally along a cofinal subnet, and the full-sequence identified
interacting theorem is reduced to a single scalar characteristic-functional limit, which
remains open.

\subsection*{Standing scope}

Everything in this manuscript is restricted to the weak-coupling region
$0\le\lambda/m_0^2<\varepsilon_{\mathscr P}$ of \S\ref{subsec:weak}; no claim is made for
the full noncritical region. The external residue --- a second-reader verification of the
GJS page images --- is recorded in Appendix~\ref{app:provenance}.

% ==================================================================
\appendix

\section{$h$-uniformity: source audit and analytic prerequisites}\label{app:huniform}

% src: m4/r1-check.tex \S\S3-5, transcribed; V(h) -> \mathcal U_h, class C -> C_E, compact set K -> Lambda;
% introduction edited 31 October 2026 (re-sourcing of A8(vi)).
This appendix has two sharply separated purposes.  First, it records the audit of the
source mechanism that preceded the re-sourcing of Assumption~\ref{A:gjs}(vi) on
31 October 2026 (items M1--M5, with the correction M6 added that day); that item was
retired on 7 November 2026, and the audit is kept as a record.  Second, it proves
the $h$-sensitive analytic prerequisites over the full class $\CE$ that
Appendix~\ref{app:comparison} consumes: normalization of $dq_h$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:Z}), the
ultraviolet limit (Lemma~\ref{lem:uv}) and uniform $L^p$ stability under the free measure
(Theorem~\ref{thm:stability}); since 7 November 2026 these are load-bearing for
Theorems~\ref{thm:D3}--\ref{thm:D4} and hence for Theorem~\ref{thm:MAIN}, and every input
they use is derived here, in Appendix~\ref{app:comparison} (Lemmas~\ref{aux:notation-kernel},
\ref{aux:notation-ident}(i) and~\ref{aux:d1-cov}, which do not use this appendix), or imported
in Assumptions~\ref{A:cutoff}, \ref{A:fkn}, \ref{A:stability} and~\ref{A:hyper} (the V3 audit of 2 November 2026 found them true but resting on three
unimported inputs, repaired on 7 November 2026; Appendix~\ref{app:provenance}).  The
appendix does \emph{not} reproduce the polymer expansion, its convergence summations, or
the proof of the cluster-expansion constants: Theorem 1.1.7's own statement makes its
constants independent of $h$; its printed proof consumes Theorem 1.1.8 at the same $h$ and
reaches sharp-time monomials only across strips parallel to the time-zero line (Scope
paragraph of Assumption~\ref{A:gjs}, point (b); item A8(iv)-T).

\subsection*{Mechanism audit}

The expansion machinery of \cite[\S\S2--4]{GJS} manipulates integrals
$\int R(\Phi)\,B\,e^{-\mathcal U_h}\,d\mu_{0,C}$, where $C$ is an interpolated
covariance and $d\mu_{0,C}$ denotes the Gaussian measure with covariance $C$, through
six devices; the following lists every point of contact with $h$ (item M6 was added on
31 October 2026, after the council of \texttt{ugjs/PLAN.md} found it missing).
% src: m4/r1-check.tex \S3, mechanisms 1-5, transcribed; M1, M2, M3, M4, M5 edited and M6 added
% 31 October 2026 (GJS pp. 600, 601, 611-613, 616, 622, 624, 625 read on page images).

\paragraph{M1 (the norms are kernel norms).}
The norm (2.4.3) of an expansion term $m_\alpha$ is
$\int du\,ds\,\exp[\cdots]\,\|w(\alpha,u,s,y,\cdot)\|_{L^2}$ times combinatorial weights
(2.4.4)--(2.4.5) built from $m_0$, $\varepsilon$, $K_2$, $N(\Delta)$ (the source's script
$\mathcal K_2$ of (2.4.4) is $e^{-(1-\varepsilon)m_0\sum\delta n}$, not the constant $K_2$),
times $K_1^{\nu}e^{\gamma t}$ ((2.4.3), p.~613): a functional of the \emph{kernel} $w$ alone. The norm itself contains
no Gaussian expectation; the kernels $w$ it is applied to are, however, defined through
integrals of $e^{-V(Y_j)}$ under the interpolated measures ((2.3.4), (2.3.8),
pp.~611--612), see M6. The norm-decrease Theorems 2.4.1, 3.1, 4.1 are inequalities between such kernel
functionals, and their proofs (Lemmas 2.4.2--2.4.4, (3.2)--(3.6), \S4) estimate kernels:
contraction kernels $\delta C$ against vertex kernels.

\paragraph{M2 (covariances are $h$-free).}
The interpolation $C(s)=sC'+(1-s)C''$ (2.0.1) is between covariances of the family
generated from $C_0$ by ``diagonalization'' with respect to unions $Y$ of unit squares,
$C_Y=YCY+(1-Y)C(1-Y)$, and convex sums (p.~600); the kernels (2.2.2)--(2.2.3) are built from $(-d^2/dx^2+m_0^2)$ and contour
geometry. Verified against the legible scan (\texttt{m4/r1-check.tex}): no $h$ enters
any covariance, any contraction kernel, or any of the Propositions 2.1.$x$.

\paragraph{M3 ($h$ in vertices, majorised at first contact).}
Vertices arise from functional derivatives of the spectator:
\[
  \frac{\delta}{\delta\Phi(x)}e^{-\mathcal U_h}
  =-\lambda\,h(x)\,{:}\mathscr P'(\Phi(x)){:}\;e^{-\mathcal U_h},
\]
and iterates with $\mathscr P'',\dots$ A vertex therefore contributes to the \emph{kernel}
a factor $\lambda h(x)p_j$ supported in a lattice square. The very first estimate applied
to it is the printed one (p.~626): ``$v(x)$ is $O(\lambda)$ times the characteristic
function of a lattice square'' --- that is, $|\lambda h(x)p_j|\le O(\lambda)\chi_\Delta(x)$,
using $0\le h\le1$ and nothing else. Downstream of this line the vertex kernels are
$h$-free. This is one of two points where $h$ touches a quantitative estimate; the other is
M6.

\paragraph{M4 (the identities are exact and $h$-blind).}
The devices (2.0.4)--(2.0.5) (covariance interpolation), (2.2.1), (2.2.5), (2.2.7)
(advancing annihilators), and (4.1)--(4.2), (4.5) (integration by parts) are identities
applied to integrands $F=R(\Phi)B_Te^{-\mathcal U_h}$; $e^{-\mathcal U_h}$ rides along
inside $F$. Their validity requires only that the integrals exist --- integrability, not
bounds. For the free-measure endpoint this is supplied, uniformly over $\CE$, by
Theorem~\ref{thm:stability}; existence under the interpolated measures $d\mu_{0,C}$ is
internal to the printed proof.  A quantitative stability bound under those measures does
enter the constants: see M6.

\paragraph{M5 (the final division adds no stability constant).}
The full expansion (4.3) reads
\[
  \int AB_T e^{-\mathcal U}d\mu_{0,C_0}
  =\sum_i\int\Bigl[\int dy\,w_i(y){:}\Phi(y_1)\cdots\Phi(y_i){:}\Bigr]B_T
   e^{-\mathcal U}d\mu_{0,C_0}
  +\sum_r w_r\int e^{-\mathcal U}d\mu_{0,C_0},
\]
with $w_i,w_r$ \emph{numerical kernels} controlled by the norm inequalities; the
remainder terms are literally multiples of $\int e^{-\mathcal U}d\mu_0$ (their Table~1:
final-$r$ terms have ``no vertices, no annihilators''). The proof of Theorems
1.1.8/1.1.11 then \emph{divides (4.3) by $\int e^{-\mathcal U}d\mu_{0,C_0}$} (p.~628):
the first sum reassembles into $dq_h$-integrals of the comparison polynomials, and the
remainder becomes $\sum_rw_r$ exactly. Every factor $e^{-\mathcal U_h}$ cancels against
the normalisation. Consequently the final division introduces no stability constant; the
stability bound that does enter the constants is the one of M6, upstream of the division.

\paragraph{M6 (stability under the interpolated measures; added 31 October 2026).}
% src: GJS pp. 622 and 624 (PDF pages 39 and 41), read on page images on 30 October 2026;
% p. 601 (PDF 18) for V(Yh) in (2.0.2); pp. 611-613 (PDF 28-30) for (2.3.4), (2.3.8), (2.4.3);
% p. 616 (PDF 33) for the order of constants; p. 625 (PDF 42) for Vol; read 31 October 2026.
The kernels $w''$ of (2.3.8) are bounded on p.~624 through a stability estimate under the
interpolated measures: ``For $m_0\ge1$, $\lambda\le1$, there is a constant $K_3$,
independent of $C$, $m_0$ and $\lambda$ such that
\[
 \textup{(2.4.34)}\qquad 1\le\|e^{-V(Y)}\|_{L_2(d\Phi_C)}\le e^{K_3|Y|}
\]
and (2.4.35) [a bound on $\|P_l''(u,s,y,x_B)\|_{L_2(d\Phi_C)}$ by
$\prod_\Delta K_3^{N(\Delta)}N(\Delta)!$ times an $L_2$ kernel norm]. These are standard
estimates for such integrals (see [1]).'' The bound ``yields (2.4.28), for $K_2\ge K_3^2$''
(p.~624), and Lemma 2.4.6 (p.~622) takes ``$K_2=K_2(m_0,K_3)$''; so $K_3$ enters every
constant chosen after $K_2$ in the printed order ``$m_0$, $K_2$, $K_1$, and then $\lambda$'' (p.~616), $C_7$ and
$C_8$ included.  The cutoff sits inside $V(Y)$: (2.0.2) on p.~601 factors $V(h)$ as
$V(Yh)+V((1-Y)h)$.  Reference [1] is Dimock--Glimm, Adv.\ Math.\ 12 (1974), not consulted
here; $K_3$ is printed independent of $C$, $m_0$ and $\lambda$, and $h$ is not mentioned.
Theorem~\ref{thm:stability} does not supply (2.4.34): it is a bound under $d\mu_0$ for a
fixed compact region, without linear growth in $|Y|$.  The convergence argument on
pp.~624--625 also uses $|m_\alpha|\le\|m_\alpha\|_{2K_2}e^{O(\mathrm{Vol})}$, ``where the
volume of interaction, denoted Vol, is the area of the set of points within unit distance
of suppt $h$. Here we use (2.4.34), (2.4.35) and a Schwarz inequality to factor out
vertices'' (p.~625), a per-$h$ qualitative step.  Items
M1 and M3--M5, as transcribed from \texttt{m4/r1-check.tex} (Gate R1, 9 August 2026),
declared this input absent; that statement was wrong about the source.

\begin{proposition}[consequence of the audited source mechanisms]\label{prop:struct}
% src: m4/r1-check.tex prop:struct, transcribed; statement narrowed 31 October 2026.
Within mechanisms M1--M5 as recorded above, and excluding the stability input of M6,
which lies outside them, every displayed quantitative bound is unchanged when $h$ ranges
over $0\le h\le1$: M3 replaces its only vertex occurrence by $|h|\le1$, while M4--M5 use
identities or exact cancellation.  Consequently the constants visible in those mechanisms
depend on $(m_0,K_2,K_1,\lambda,n,\varepsilon,\mathscr P)$ and not on $h$; the
$h$-uniformity of $K_3$ in M6 is not among the audited mechanisms.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
By direct substitution in M1--M5: the quantitative chain displayed there is entirely at the level of kernel norms (M1), whose
ingredients are $h$-free covariance/contraction kernels (M2) and vertex kernels majorised
by $O(\lambda)\chi_\Delta$ (M3); the measure-level steps are exact identities (M4) or
exact cancellations (M5).  This proposition is deliberately limited to the audited
mechanisms; it makes no completeness claim about portions of the source expansion not
reproduced here.
\end{proof}

\subsection*{The two $h$-sensitive analytic facts, proved}

% Notation paragraph and Lemmas C.2-C.5 repaired 7 November 2026 (repairs R1-R10 and items
% m-1 to m-9 of the V3 audit, ugjs/council/v3-appendix-c-report.md): every analytic input is
% now derived or imported, every constant is explicit, every threshold is an inequality.
Notation for this subsection: $G:=(-\Delta_{\R^2}+m_0^2)^{-1}$ is the Euclidean
covariance, with the kernel
\[
 G(\zeta)=\int_0^\infty\frac{e^{-m_0^2t-|\zeta|^2/(4t)}}{4\pi t}\,dt\qquad(\zeta\ne0)
\]
of Lemma~\ref{aux:notation-kernel}(v) (Appendix~\ref{app:comparison}): positive, radial,
nonincreasing in $|\zeta|$, continuous on $\R^2\setminus\{0\}$, with
$\int_{\R^2}G\,d\zeta=m_0^{-2}$ and
\begin{equation}
 0<G(\zeta)\le\frac{\ell(\zeta)}{4\pi},\qquad
 \ell(\zeta):=b_0+2\bigl|\log|\zeta|\bigr|,\qquad
 b_0:=\log4+e^{-1}+m_0^{-2}
 \tag{C.a}\label{eq:Glog}
\end{equation}
(the same lemma, part (v), through its part (iii); the substitution $t=(|\zeta|/2m_0)e^{u}$ writes
$G(\zeta)=(2\pi)^{-1}K_0(m_0|\zeta|)$ with $K_0(x):=\int_0^\infty e^{-x\cosh u}\,du$,
which is not used below).  Further, for $\zeta\ne0$,
\begin{equation}
 |\nabla G(\zeta)|\le\frac1{2\pi|\zeta|} .
 \tag{C.b}\label{eq:Ggrad}
\end{equation}
Indeed, on $\{|\zeta|\ge\varepsilon\}$, $\varepsilon>0$, the $\zeta$-derivative of the
integrand is dominated by $(4\pi t)^{-1}(|\zeta|/2t)e^{-\varepsilon^2/(4t)}$ for $|\zeta|$
in a bounded set, which is integrable over $t\in(0,\infty)$, so differentiation under the
integral gives
\[
 \nabla G(\zeta)=-\int_0^\infty\frac{\zeta}{2t}\,\frac{e^{-m_0^2t-|\zeta|^2/(4t)}}{4\pi t}\,dt ,
 \qquad
 |\nabla G(\zeta)|\le\frac{|\zeta|}{8\pi}\int_0^\infty t^{-2}e^{-|\zeta|^2/(4t)}\,dt
 =\frac{|\zeta|}{8\pi}\cdot\frac4{|\zeta|^2}
\]
(substitute $u=|\zeta|^2/(4t)$, so that $t^{-2}\,dt=-4|\zeta|^{-2}\,du$ and the integral is
$4|\zeta|^{-2}\int_0^\infty e^{-u}du$).
Let $h\in\CE$ with $\supp h\subseteq\Lambda$, $\Lambda$ compact, and $R:=\operatorname{diam}
\Lambda$ (so $|\Lambda|\le\pi R^2$); $\mathcal U_h=\lambda\sum_{n=0}^{2p'}a_n{:}\Phi^n(h){:}$
and $Z(h)=\int e^{-\mathcal U_h}d\mu_0$ as in Assumption~\ref{A:stability}, whose
construction paragraph supplies the chaoses $\Gamma_n:=\Gamma_n(\HH_G)$, the pairing
\eqref{eq:wickpairingE}, the isometry \eqref{eq:wickisometry}, the orthogonality of the
chaoses and the action of $\Gamma(e^{-t}\mathbf 1)$ on them.  Choose once and
for all
\[
 \delta\in C_c^\infty(\R^2),\qquad
 \delta\ge0,\qquad \delta(z)=\delta(-z),\qquad
 \supp\delta\subseteq\{z:|z|\le1\},\qquad
 \int_{\R^2}\delta(z)\,dz=1,
\]
and, for $\kappa\ge1$, define the two-dimensional rescaling
$\delta_\kappa(z):=\kappa^2\delta(\kappa z)$, so that $\|\delta_\kappa\|_\infty=\kappa^2
\|\delta\|_\infty$, $\|\delta_\kappa\|_1=1$ and $\supp\delta_\kappa\subseteq\{|z|\le1/\kappa\}$;
$\|\delta\|_\infty\ge1/\pi$, since $\delta$ is a probability density on the unit disc.  Thus
convolution by $\delta_\kappa$ is a positive average over a ball of radius $\kappa^{-1}$; a
double convolution averages over displacements of radius at most $2\kappa^{-1}$.  Set
$\Phi_\kappa(z):=\Phi(\delta_\kappa(z-\cdot))$, a Gaussian random variable for each $z$, with
$z\mapsto\Phi_\kappa(z)(q)=(q*\delta_\kappa)(z)$ smooth for every $q\in\mathcal S'(\R^2)$,
and variance
\[
 c_\kappa:=\E[\Phi_\kappa(z)^2]=\int(\delta_\kappa*\delta_\kappa)(w)\,G(w)\,dw ,
\]
which does not depend on $z$ for $\kappa\ge2$, the only range used below
(Lemma~\ref{aux:d1-cov}(iii): $c_\kappa=G_{\kappa\kappa}(0)$).  For every
$\kappa\ge2$,
\begin{equation}
 c_\kappa\le\frac1{2\pi}\log\kappa+C_*,\qquad
 C_*:=\frac{b_0+\pi\|\delta\|_\infty}{4\pi} .
 \tag{C.c}\label{eq:ckappa}
\end{equation}
Indeed $\rho:=\delta_\kappa*\delta_\kappa$ is a probability density with $\supp\rho\subseteq
\{|w|\le2/\kappa\}$ and $\|\rho\|_\infty\le\|\delta_\kappa\|_\infty\|\delta_\kappa\|_1=\kappa^2
\|\delta\|_\infty$; on $\supp\rho$, $|w|\le2/\kappa\le1$, so \eqref{eq:Glog} gives
$4\pi c_\kappa\le\int\rho(w)\bigl(b_0+2\log\tfrac1{|w|}\bigr)dw=b_0+2\log\kappa+2\int\rho(w)
\log\tfrac1{\kappa|w|}\,dw$, and, dropping the negative part of the last logarithm,
$\int\rho(w)\log_+\tfrac1{\kappa|w|}\,dw\le\kappa^2\|\delta\|_\infty\int_{|w|\le1/\kappa}\log
\tfrac1{\kappa|w|}\,dw=\|\delta\|_\infty\int_{|v|\le1}\log\tfrac1{|v|}\,dv=2\pi\|\delta\|_\infty
\int_0^1s\log\tfrac1s\,ds=\tfrac\pi2\|\delta\|_\infty$ (substitute $w=v/\kappa$; then polar
coordinates; $\int_0^1s\log\frac1s\,ds=\frac14$).  Let $\operatorname{He}_n$ be the Hermite
polynomials with generating function $\exp(\alpha y-\frac12\alpha^2)=\sum_n\alpha^n
\operatorname{He}_n(y)/n!$, so that, for $c\ge0$,
\[
 {:}y^n{:}_c:=c^{n/2}\operatorname{He}_n(y/\sqrt c)=\sum_{0\le j\le n/2}\frac{n!}{j!\,(n-2j)!}
 \Bigl(-\frac c2\Bigr)^jy^{n-2j}
\]
(the coefficient of $\alpha^n/n!$ in $\exp(\alpha y-\frac12\alpha^2c)$; the sum also defines
it at $c=0$, where ${:}y^n{:}_0=y^n$), and ${:}\mathscr P{:}_c(y):=\sum_na_n{:}y^n{:}_c$; for a
centred Gaussian variable $X$ of variance $c$ this is the Wick power of
Assumption~\ref{A:cutoff}(a), by (I.18a)--(I.18b).  Put
\[
 \mathcal U_{h,\kappa}:=\lambda\int h(z)\,{:}\mathscr P(\Phi_\kappa(z)){:}_{c_\kappa}\,dz ,
\]
the integral taken pointwise in $q$ (the integrand is continuous in $z$ and $h$ is
integrable with bounded support).  Its degree-$n$ part
$X_{n,\kappa}:=\int h(z)\,{:}\Phi_\kappa(z)^n{:}_{c_\kappa}\,dz$ lies in $\Gamma_n$ and equals the
$L^2(d\mu_0)$-valued integral of the same integrand (Lemma~\ref{aux:notation-ident}(i),
Appendix~\ref{app:comparison}: it is the $L^2$-limit of Riemann sums of the Wick monomials
${:}\Phi(\delta_\kappa(z-\cdot))^n{:}$), so inner products with it are computed under the
integral sign; its degree-$0$ part is $\lambda a_0\int h$, the same as that of
$\mathcal U_h$.  By Lemma~\ref{lem:uv} below and the comparison in the proof of
Theorem~\ref{thm:stability}, $\mathcal U_{h,\kappa}\to\mathcal U_h$ in every $L^p(d\mu_0)$
as $\kappa\to\infty$; this is used only after Theorem~\ref{thm:stability}.  (The
constants $c_1$, $c_2$ of Lemmas~\ref{lem:wick} and~\ref{lem:uv} are not values of
$\kappa\mapsto c_\kappa$.)

\begin{lemma}[normalisation]\label{lem:Z}
% src: m4/r1-check.tex lem:Z, transcribed.
For every $h\in\CE$ with $\supp h\subseteq\Lambda$:
$Z(h)\ge e^{-\lambda|a_0|\,|\Lambda|}>0$, and $Z(h)<\infty$ by
Theorem~\ref{thm:stability}. Hence $dq_h$ is a well-defined probability measure on the
whole class $\CE$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
$\mathcal U_h\in L^1(d\mu_0)$ by Assumption~\ref{A:stability}.  For $n\ge1$,
${:}\Phi^n(h){:}\in\Gamma_n$ is orthogonal to the constants $\Gamma_0$ (orthogonality of
the chaoses, Assumption~\ref{A:cutoff}(c)), so $\E[{:}\Phi^n(h){:}]=\ip1{{:}\Phi^n(h){:}}=0$;
hence $\E[\mathcal U_h]=\lambda a_0\int h\in[-\lambda|a_0||\Lambda|,\lambda|a_0||\Lambda|]$,
since $0\le h\le\mathbf 1_\Lambda$ (both $h\ge0$ and $h\le1$ are used here), $\lambda\ge0$
and $|\Lambda|$ is the Lebesgue measure.  Jensen's inequality for the convex function
$\exp$ and the probability measure $d\mu_0$ gives
$Z(h)\ge e^{-\E[\mathcal U_h]}\ge e^{-\lambda|a_0||\Lambda|}$ (the inequality holds in
$(0,\infty]$ even before finiteness is known).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Wick lower bound; the $h\ge0$ entry]\label{lem:wick}
% src: m4/r1-check.tex lem:wick, transcribed; constants derived 7 November 2026 (repair R7).
With
\begin{gather*}
 C(\mathscr P):=\sum_{n=0}^{2p'}|a_n|\max_{0\le j\le n/2}\frac{n!}{j!\,(n-2j)!\,2^j},\\
 C_{p'}:=\max\Bigl\{1,\ \max_{1\le m<2p'}\frac{2p'-m}{2p'}\,(2m)^{m/(2p'-m)}\Bigr\},
\end{gather*}
\[
 b(\mathscr P):=C_{p'}\sum_{m=0}^{2p'-1}C(\mathscr P)^{2p'/(2p'-m)}\,a_{2p'}^{-m/(2p'-m)} ,
\]
one has ${:}\mathscr P{:}_c(x)\ge-b(\mathscr P)\,(1+c)^{p'}$ for all $c\ge0$ and $x\in\R$.
Consequently, for every $h\in\CE$ with $\supp h\subseteq\Lambda$ and every $\kappa\ge2$,
\[
  \mathcal U_{h,\kappa}\ \ge\
  -\lambda\,b(\mathscr P)\,|\Lambda|\,\bigl(1+C_*+\tfrac1{2\pi}\log\kappa\bigr)^{p'}
  \ \ge\ -c_1\,(\log\kappa)^{p'},
\]
\[
  c_1:=\max\Bigl\{1,\ \lambda\,b(\mathscr P)\,|\Lambda|\Bigl(\frac{1+C_*}{\log2}+\frac1{2\pi}
  \Bigr)^{p'}\Bigr\}>0 ,
\]
with $C_*$ of \eqref{eq:ckappa}; $c_1$ depends on $\lambda$, $\mathscr P$, $|\Lambda|$, $m_0$
and $\|\delta\|_\infty$ (the last two through $C_*$), and the maximum with $1$ makes
$c_1\ge1$, which the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:stability} uses (for its threshold $b_1$).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By the expansion of ${:}y^n{:}_c$ in the notation paragraph,
${:}\mathscr P{:}_c(x)=\sum_{m=0}^{2p'}\beta_mx^m$ with $\beta_{2p'}=a_{2p'}=:A>0$ and, for
$m<2p'$, $\beta_m=\sum_{n\equiv m\ (2),\ n\ge m}a_n\frac{n!}{j!\,m!}(-\frac c2)^j$ with
$j=(n-m)/2\le(2p'-m)/2$; since $c^j\le(1+c)^j\le(1+c)^{(2p'-m)/2}$,
$|\beta_m|\le C(\mathscr P)(1+c)^{(2p'-m)/2}$.  For $1\le m<2p'$, Young's inequality
$uv\le u^P/P+v^{P'}/P'$ with $P:=2p'/m$, $P':=2p'/(2p'-m)$ (so $1/P+1/P'=1$), applied to
$u:=\eta|x|^m$, $v:=|\beta_m|/\eta$ with $\eta^{2p'/m}:=A/(2m)$, gives
\begin{align*}
  |\beta_m||x|^m&\le\frac{m}{2p'}\,\eta^{2p'/m}x^{2p'}+\frac{2p'-m}{2p'}\Bigl(\frac{|\beta_m|}
  {\eta}\Bigr)^{2p'/(2p'-m)}\\
  &=\frac{A}{4p'}\,x^{2p'}+\frac{2p'-m}{2p'}(2m)^{m/(2p'-m)}\Bigl(\frac{|\beta_m|^{2p'}}{A^{m}}
  \Bigr)^{1/(2p'-m)},
\end{align*}
and for $m=0$ trivially $|\beta_0|\le1\cdot(|\beta_0|^{2p'}/A^0)^{1/2p'}$.  Summing over the
$2p'$ values $m<2p'$, the $x^{2p'}$ terms total at most $\frac{A}{2}x^{2p'}$, so
${:}\mathscr P{:}_c(x)\ge\frac A2x^{2p'}-C_{p'}\sum_{m<2p'}(|\beta_m|^{2p'}/A^m)^{1/(2p'-m)}$.
With $|\beta_m|\le C(\mathscr P)(1+c)^{(2p'-m)/2}$ the exponent of $(1+c)$ is
$\frac{2p'}{2p'-m}\cdot\frac{2p'-m}{2}=p'$, uniformly in $m$, which gives the first claim
with the displayed $b(\mathscr P)$.  For the second: $h\ge0$ (this is where the sign of $h$
is used), so the pointwise bound at $c=c_\kappa$ integrates to $\mathcal U_{h,\kappa}\ge
-\lambda b(\mathscr P)(1+c_\kappa)^{p'}\int h\ge-\lambda b(\mathscr P)|\Lambda|(1+c_\kappa)^{p'}$,
using $\int h\le|\Lambda|$ ($h\le1$); then \eqref{eq:ckappa}; and finally
$1+C_*+\frac1{2\pi}\log\kappa\le\bigl(\frac{1+C_*}{\log2}+\frac1{2\pi}\bigr)\log\kappa$ for
$\kappa\ge2$, since $\log\kappa\ge\log2$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[ultraviolet difference; the $h\le1$ entry]\label{lem:uv}
% src: m4/r1-check.tex lem:uv, transcribed; kernel facts derived and constant made explicit
% 7 November 2026 (repairs R1-R5, R9 of the V3 audit; the gradient bound (C.3) replaces R3).
For $n\ge1$ and $R\ge0$ put
\begin{gather*}
 A_n:=16\pi\Bigl[(4\pi)^{-n}2^{n-1}\bigl((b_0+2)^n+2^{n+1}n!\bigr)+3\bigl(\|\delta\|_\infty
 (b_0+2)\bigr)^n\Bigr],\\
 L_m(R):=\int_0^R\bigl(b_0+2+2|\log r|\bigr)^m\,dr\ \ (m\ge0),\qquad
 B_n(R):=12\,n\,(4\pi)^{-(n-1)}L_{n-1}(R),\\
 c_2:=\lambda^2|\Lambda|\sum_{n=1}^{2p'}a_n^2\,n!\Bigl[\frac{A_n}2\Bigl(1+\frac1{\log2}\Bigr)^n
 (\log2)^{n-2p'}+B_n(R)\,(\log2)^{-2p'}\Bigr] .
\end{gather*}
Then for all $h\in\CE$ with $\supp h\subseteq\Lambda$, $R=\operatorname{diam}\Lambda$, and all
$\kappa\ge2$,
\[
  \bigl\|\mathcal U_h-\mathcal U_{h,\kappa}\bigr\|_{L^2(d\mu_0)}^2
  \ \le\ c_2\,\kappa^{-1}(\log\kappa)^{2p'} .
\]
The constant $c_2$ depends on $\lambda$, $\mathscr P$, $|\Lambda|$, $\operatorname{diam}
\Lambda$, $m_0$ (through $b_0$) and $\|\delta\|_\infty$, and on nothing else; every
$L_m(R)$ is finite, the logarithmic singularity being integrable.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\emph{Step 1: chaos decomposition and isometry.}  Write $G^{(1)}_\kappa:=\delta_\kappa*G$ and
$G_\kappa:=\delta_\kappa*\delta_\kappa*G$, both continuous on $\R^2$ as convolutions of the
locally integrable $G$ with $C_c^\infty$ functions (cf.\ Lemma~\ref{aux:d1-cov}(iii)).  With
$X_{n,\kappa}$ as in the notation paragraph,
$\mathcal U_h-\mathcal U_{h,\kappa}=\lambda\sum_{n=1}^{2p'}a_n\bigl({:}\Phi^n(h){:}-X_{n,\kappa}
\bigr)$ (the degree-$0$ terms cancel), each summand lying in $\Gamma_n$; by the
orthogonality of the chaoses,
$\|\mathcal U_h-\mathcal U_{h,\kappa}\|_2^2=\lambda^2\sum_na_n^2\|{:}\Phi^n(h){:}-X_{n,\kappa}
\|_2^2$.  Expanding the square: $\|{:}\Phi^n(h){:}\|_2^2=n!\iint h(z)h(z')G(z-z')^n$ is
\eqref{eq:wickisometry};
\[
 \ip{{:}\Phi^n(h){:}}{X_{n,\kappa}}=\int h(z)\,\ip{{:}\Phi^n(h){:}}{{:}\Phi_\kappa(z)^n{:}}\,dz
\]
(the inner product passes under the $L^2$-valued integral), and
for the Wick monomial ${:}\Phi_\kappa(z)^n{:}={:}\Phi(f)\cdots\Phi(f){:}$ with $f=\delta_\kappa
(z-\cdot)$ the pairing \eqref{eq:wickpairingE} gives $n!\int\bigl(\int\delta_\kappa(z-x)G(x-y)
\,dx\bigr)^nh(y)\,dy=n!\int G^{(1)}_\kappa(z-y)^nh(y)\,dy$; and $\ip{{:}\Phi_\kappa(z)^n{:}}
{{:}\Phi_\kappa(z')^n{:}}=n!\,\E[\Phi_\kappa(z)\Phi_\kappa(z')]^n=n!\,G_\kappa(z-z')^n$ by
(I.21) and Lemma~\ref{aux:d1-cov}(iii), so $\|X_{n,\kappa}\|_2^2=n!\iint h(z)h(z')G_\kappa
(z-z')^n$.  Hence
\[
  \|\mathcal U_h-\mathcal U_{h,\kappa}\|_{L^2}^2
  =\lambda^2\sum_{n=1}^{2p'}a_n^2\,n!\iint h(z)h(z')\,
  \bigl[G^n-2(G^{(1)}_\kappa)^n+G_\kappa^n\bigr](z-z')\,dz\,dz' .
\]
Using $|h|\le\mathbf 1_\Lambda$ (this is where the upper bound $h\le1$ is used) and
$|z-z'|\le R$ for $z,z'\in\Lambda$, the double integral is at most
$|\Lambda|\int_{|\zeta|\le R}\bigl|G^n-2J_1^n+J_2^n\bigr|(\zeta)\,d\zeta$ with
$J_1:=G^{(1)}_\kappa$, $J_2:=G_\kappa$.

\emph{Step 2: three facts about the averages.}  Let $J=\nu*G$ with $\nu\in\{\delta_\kappa,
\delta_\kappa*\delta_\kappa\}$: a probability density with $\supp\nu\subseteq\{|w|\le2/\kappa\}$
and $\|\nu\|_\infty\le\kappa^2\|\delta\|_\infty$.
\emph{(J1)} $J(\zeta)\le\|\delta\|_\infty(b_0+1+2\log\kappa)\le\|\delta\|_\infty(b_0+2)(1+\log
\kappa)$ for every $\zeta$.  Indeed $J(\zeta)\le\|\nu\|_\infty\int_{B(\zeta,2/\kappa)}G\le
\kappa^2\|\delta\|_\infty\int_{B(0,2/\kappa)}G$, because for the radial nonincreasing $G$ and
$a>0$ one has $\int_{B(\zeta,a)}G\le\int_{B(0,a)}G$ (the sets $B(\zeta,a)\setminus B(0,a)$ and
$B(0,a)\setminus B(\zeta,a)$ have equal measure, $G\le G|_{|\cdot|=a}$ on the first and
$G\ge G|_{|\cdot|=a}$ on the second); with $a:=2/\kappa\le1$, \eqref{eq:Glog} and polar
coordinates, $\int_{B(0,a)}G\le\frac1{4\pi}\,2\pi\int_0^ar\bigl(b_0+2\log\tfrac1r\bigr)dr
=\frac{a^2}4\bigl(b_0+1+2\log\tfrac1a\bigr)=\kappa^{-2}\bigl(b_0+1+2\log\tfrac\kappa2\bigr)$,
and $(b_0+2)(1+\log\kappa)-(b_0+1+2\log\kappa)=1+b_0\log\kappa\ge0$.
\emph{(J2)} For $|\zeta|\ge4/\kappa$: $|J(\zeta)-G(\zeta)|\le\frac2{\pi\kappa|\zeta|}$.  Indeed
$J(\zeta)-G(\zeta)=\int\nu(w)\bigl(G(\zeta-w)-G(\zeta)\bigr)dw$, and for $|w|\le2/\kappa\le
|\zeta|/2$ the segment from $\zeta$ to $\zeta-w$ lies in $\{|\xi|\ge|\zeta|/2\}$, where
$|\nabla G(\xi)|\le(\pi|\zeta|)^{-1}$ by \eqref{eq:Ggrad}; the mean value theorem gives
$|G(\zeta-w)-G(\zeta)|\le|w|/(\pi|\zeta|)\le2/(\pi\kappa|\zeta|)$.
\emph{(J3)} For $|\zeta|\ge4/\kappa$: $\max\{G,J\}(\zeta)\le\frac1{4\pi}\bigl(\ell(\zeta)+2
\bigr)$, since by (J2) and $\kappa|\zeta|\ge4$, $J\le G+\frac1{2\pi}$, and $\frac1{2\pi}=
\frac2{4\pi}$.

\emph{Step 3: the region $|\zeta|<a$, $a:=4/\kappa\le2$.}  By (J1), $\int_{|\zeta|<a}J^n\le
\pi a^2\bigl(\|\delta\|_\infty(b_0+2)\bigr)^n(1+\log\kappa)^n=16\pi\kappa^{-2}\bigl(\|\delta\|_
\infty(b_0+2)\bigr)^n(1+\log\kappa)^n$ for $J=J_1,J_2$.  For $G$: on $|\zeta|\le2$,
$|\log|\zeta||\le\log2+\log_+\frac1{|\zeta|}$, so $\ell(\zeta)\le(b_0+2)+2\log_+\frac1{|\zeta|}$
and, by convexity, $\ell^n\le2^{n-1}\bigl[(b_0+2)^n+2^n(\log_+\frac1{|\zeta|})^n\bigr]$; and
$\int_{|\zeta|<a}(\log_+\frac1{|\zeta|})^n\,d\zeta=2\pi\int_0^{\min\{a,1\}}r(\log\tfrac1r)^n\,dr
\le2\pi\cdot16\kappa^{-2}(1+\log\kappa)^n\,n!$.  (For $\kappa\ge4$, i.e.\ $a\le1$: the
substitution $r=ae^{-s}$ gives $\int_0^ar(\log\frac1r)^ndr=a^2\sum_{k=0}^n\binom nk(\log\tfrac1a
)^{n-k}\frac{k!}{2^{k+1}}\le a^2(1+\log\tfrac1a)^n\sum_k\frac{n!}{(n-k)!\,2^{k+1}}\le a^2(1+\log
\tfrac1a)^nn!$, with $a^2=16/\kappa^2$ and $\log\frac1a=\log\frac\kappa4\le\log\kappa$; for
$2\le\kappa<4$: $\int_0^1r(\log\frac1r)^ndr=n!/2^{n+1}\le n!\le16\kappa^{-2}n!$, as $\kappa^2<16$.)
Hence, with $\pi a^2=16\pi\kappa^{-2}$,
$\int_{|\zeta|<a}G^n\le(4\pi)^{-n}\int_{|\zeta|<a}\ell^n\le(4\pi)^{-n}2^{n-1}\,16\pi\kappa^{-2}
(1+\log\kappa)^n\bigl[(b_0+2)^n+2^{n+1}n!\bigr]$, and altogether
\[
  \int_{|\zeta|<a}\bigl[G^n+2J_1^n+J_2^n\bigr]\le\kappa^{-2}(1+\log\kappa)^n\,A_n .
\]

\emph{Step 4: the region $a\le|\zeta|\le R$} (empty if $R<a$).  There
$|G^n-2J_1^n+J_2^n|\le2|G^n-J_1^n|+|G^n-J_2^n|$, and for $J=J_1,J_2$, by (J2)--(J3),
$|G^n-J^n|\le n\max\{G,J\}^{n-1}|G-J|\le n(4\pi)^{-(n-1)}(\ell+2)^{n-1}\cdot2(\pi\kappa|\zeta|)^{-1}$.
In polar coordinates, $|\zeta|=r$,
\begin{align*}
  \int_{a\le|\zeta|\le R}\bigl|G^n-2J_1^n+J_2^n\bigr|
  &\le2\pi\int_a^R3n(4\pi)^{-(n-1)}\bigl(b_0+2+2|\log r|\bigr)^{n-1}\frac{2}{\pi\kappa r}\,r\,dr\\
  &\le\frac{12n}{\kappa}(4\pi)^{-(n-1)}L_{n-1}(R)=\kappa^{-1}B_n(R) .
\end{align*}

\emph{Step 5: assembly.}  By Steps~1, 3 and~4,
$\|\mathcal U_h-\mathcal U_{h,\kappa}\|_2^2\le\lambda^2|\Lambda|\sum_na_n^2n!\bigl[\kappa^{-2}
(1+\log\kappa)^nA_n+\kappa^{-1}B_n(R)\bigr]$.  For $\kappa\ge2$ and $1\le n\le2p'$:
$1+\log\kappa\le(1+\frac1{\log2})\log\kappa$, $(\log\kappa)^n\le(\log2)^{n-2p'}(\log\kappa)^{2p'}$
(as $\log\kappa\ge\log2$ and $n-2p'\le0$), $\kappa^{-2}\le\frac12\kappa^{-1}$, and
$1\le(\log\kappa/\log2)^{2p'}$; inserting these gives $c_2\kappa^{-1}(\log\kappa)^{2p'}$.
(Tests: at $n=1$ the integrand of Step~4 is $|J_2-2J_1+G|\le3\cdot2(\pi\kappa r)^{-1}$ and
$L_0(R)=R$; the $r$-integral in Step~3 at $n=1$, $a\le1$, is $\frac{a^2}2(\frac12+\log\frac1a)
\le a^2(1+\log\frac1a)$.)
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[$h$-uniform stability]\label{thm:stability}
% src: m4/r1-check.tex thm:stability, transcribed; thresholds written as inequalities and the
% bound recorded 7 November 2026 (repair R10 and items m-4, m-5, m-6 of the V3 audit).
For every $p<\infty$ and compact $\Lambda$,
\[
  \sup\bigl\{\|e^{-\mathcal U_h}\|_{L^p(d\mu_0)}\ :\ h\in\CE,\
  \supp h\subseteq\Lambda\bigr\}\ \le\ \bigl(1+e^{pb_*}\bigr)^{1/p}<\infty ,
\]
where $b_*=b_*(p,p',c_1,c_2)$ is the threshold defined in the proof, with $c_1$ of
Lemma~\ref{lem:wick} and $c_2$ of Lemma~\ref{lem:uv}.  The bound depends on $\Lambda$ only
through $|\Lambda|$ and $\operatorname{diam}\Lambda$ (monotonically), and otherwise on
$p$, $\lambda$, $\mathscr P$, $m_0$ and $\|\delta\|_\infty$; in particular it is the same
for all translates of $\Lambda$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
(For $\lambda=0$, $\mathcal U_h=0$ and the supremum is $1$; assume $\lambda>0$.  If
$|\Lambda|=0$, every $h$ of the class vanishes almost everywhere, $\mathcal U_h=0$ and the
supremum is $1$ again; assume $|\Lambda|>0$.)
$Y:=\mathcal U_h-\mathcal U_{h,\kappa}$ is a sum $Y=\sum_{r=0}^{2p'}Y_r$ with $Y_r:=\lambda
a_r({:}\Phi^r(h){:}-X_{r,\kappa})\in\Gamma_r$ (Step~1 of the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:uv}),
so Nelson's hypercontractive moment comparison applies in the form
\[
 \|Y\|_{L^q}\le(q-1)^{p'}\|Y\|_{L^2}\qquad(q\ge2) ,
\]
which is \cite[Thm.~I.22, (I.47), p.~38]{SimonBook} with $m=2p'$ (Assumption~\ref{A:hyper},
Sources).  (Proof from the abstract form of Assumption~\ref{A:hyper} \cite[Thm.~I.17]{SimonBook}:
the second-quantized contraction $\Gamma(e^{-t})$ on $L^2(d\mu_0)$ satisfies
$\|\Gamma(e^{-t})X\|_q\le\|X\|_2$ for $q=1+e^{2t}$, and
$\Gamma(e^{-t})Y_r=e^{-rt}Y_r$ by (I.32) on the Wick monomials spanning $\Gamma_r$ and
continuity (Assumption~\ref{A:stability}, construction paragraph). Write $Y=\Gamma(e^{-t})Z$
with $Z=\sum_re^{rt}Y_r$; by orthogonality of the chaoses, $\|Z\|_2\le e^{2p't}\|Y\|_2$,
whence $\|Y\|_q\le\|Z\|_2\le e^{2p't}\|Y\|_2=(q-1)^{p'}\|Y\|_2$.)

\emph{The thresholds.}  Put
\[
 b_0':=2c_1\,(4p'\log2)^{p'},
\]
\[
 b_1:=\begin{cases}2c_1\bigl(\max\{0,\,4+2\log c_2\}\bigr)^{p'}&(c_2>0),\\
 0&(c_2=0),\end{cases}
\]
and, with $f(b):=\frac1{4p'}\bigl(\frac b{2c_1}\bigr)^{1/p'}-\log(2pb)$, which is
nondecreasing on $[2c_1(4p'^2)^{p'},\infty)$ (there $f'(b)=b^{-1}[(4p'^2)^{-1}(b/2c_1)^{1/p'}
-1]\ge0$) and tends to $+\infty$,
\[
 b_2(p):=\min\bigl\{b\ge2c_1(4p'^2)^{p'}:\ f(b)\ge0\bigr\},\qquad
 b_*:=\max\{b_0',\,b_1,\,b_2(p)\} .
\]
(The thresholds $b_0',b_1,b_2(p),b_*$ are local to this proof; the prime on $b_0'$ keeps
it apart from the kernel constant $b_0$ of \eqref{eq:Glog}, and the constant $b_1$ of
Lemma~\ref{aux:d1-cov} is unrelated.)

For $b\ge b_*$ choose $\kappa=\kappa(b)$ by $c_1(\log\kappa)^{p'}=b/2$, i.e.\
$L:=\log\kappa=(b/2c_1)^{1/p'}$, and $2q:=e^{L/(4p')}$.  Since $b\ge b_0'$, $L\ge4p'\log2$,
so $\kappa\ge2^{4p'}\ge2$ (Lemmas~\ref{lem:wick} and~\ref{lem:uv} apply) and $2q\ge2$ (the
comparison applies at the exponent $2q$).  By Lemma~\ref{lem:wick},
$\{\mathcal U_h\le-b\}\subseteq\{\mathcal U_h-\mathcal U_{h,\kappa}\le-b/2\}$, so by
Chebyshev's inequality in $L^{2q}$, the comparison and Lemma~\ref{lem:uv},
\begin{align*}
  \Pr\bigl[\mathcal U_h\le-b\bigr]
  &\le\Bigl(\frac2b\Bigr)^{2q}(2q-1)^{2p'q}\,\bigl(c_2\,e^{-L}L^{2p'}\bigr)^{q}\\
  &\le\Bigl[\frac{2\sqrt{c_2}}{b}\,(2q)^{p'}\,L^{p'}e^{-L/2}\Bigr]^{2q}
  =\Bigl[\frac{2\sqrt{c_2}}{b}\,L^{p'}e^{-L/4}\Bigr]^{2q},
\end{align*}
using $(2q)^{p'}=e^{L/4}$.  With $b=2c_1L^{p'}$ the bracket equals
$(\sqrt{c_2}/c_1)\,e^{-L/4}$, which is $\le e^{-1}$ because $b\ge b_1$ gives
$L\ge4+2\log c_2\ge4+2\log(c_2/c_1^2)$ (as $c_1\ge1$), i.e.\ $\log(\sqrt{c_2}/c_1)-L/4\le-1$
(for $c_2=0$ the bracket is $0$).  Hence
\[
  \Pr\bigl[\mathcal U_h\le-b\bigr]\ \le\ e^{-2q}
  =\exp\Bigl(-\exp\Bigl(\tfrac1{4p'}(b/2c_1)^{1/p'}\Bigr)\Bigr)\ \le\ e^{-2pb}
  \qquad(b\ge b_*),
\]
the last step because $b\ge b_2(p)$ gives $f(b)\ge0$, i.e.\
$\exp\bigl(\frac1{4p'}(b/2c_1)^{1/p'}\bigr)\ge2pb$: uniformly over the stated class.  Then,
by the layer-cake formula for the probability measure $d\mu_0$,
\[
  \|e^{-\mathcal U_h}\|_{L^p}^p
  =\int_{-\infty}^\infty pe^{pb}\Pr\bigl[-\mathcal U_h>b\bigr]\,db
  \le e^{pb_*}+\int_{b_*}^\infty pe^{-pb}\,db\le e^{pb_*}+1 ,
\]
bounding the probability by $1$ for $b<b_*$ and by $e^{-2pb}$ for $b\ge b_*$.  The
threshold $b_*$ is a function of $p$, $p'$, $c_1$ and $c_2$ alone, nondecreasing in $c_1$ and
$c_2$ ($b_0'$ and $b_1$ visibly; $b_2(p)$ because, when $c_1$ grows, $f$ decreases pointwise and
the lower end of its range grows); $c_1$ depends on $\Lambda$ through $|\Lambda|$ and $c_2$
through $|\Lambda|$ and $\operatorname{diam}\Lambda$, both nondecreasingly, which gives the
dependence stated.  Every constant above came from
Lemmas~\ref{lem:wick}--\ref{lem:uv}, which used $h\ge0$ and $h\le1$ (and compact support).
\end{proof}

Since Lemma~\ref{lem:uv} gives $\mathcal U_{h,\kappa}\to\mathcal U_h$ in $L^2(d\mu_0)$ and
the comparison above holds for $\mathcal U_h-\mathcal U_{h,\kappa}$ at every $\kappa$, the
convergence holds in every $L^q(d\mu_0)$, $q<\infty$, as the notation paragraph announced.

\begin{remark}[why the class is what it is]\label{rem:whyclass}
% src: m4/r1-check.tex, closing remark of \S4, transcribed.
The two halves of the constraint $0\le h\le1$ are used in complementary places:
positivity in the pointwise lower bound (Lemma~\ref{lem:wick} would fail for $h$ of
variable sign --- the interaction density is unbounded above \emph{and} below), and
boundedness in the $L^2$ kernel estimate (Lemma~\ref{lem:uv}) and in M3; the upper bound
also enters, through $\int h\le|\Lambda|$, in Lemmas~\ref{lem:Z} and~\ref{lem:wick}.
Nothing else about
$h$ --- smoothness, shape, time extent, monotonicity along a sequence --- is touched in
Lemmas~\ref{lem:wick}--\ref{lem:uv} or in M3; the stability input of the source's own proof
(M6) is a separate matter. This is the structural content of Source Fact~\ref{sf:printed}.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[coverage of every CE application]\label{prop:discharge}
% src: m4/r1-check.tex thm:verdict, transcribed; hypothesis clause removed 31 October 2026;
% restated 7 November 2026: every use of clustering is CE2 as printed, with the orientations.
CE1 is used nowhere in \S\S\ref{sec:slab}--\ref{sec:G}: every use of Euclidean clustering
(CE2) in this manuscript is Source Fact~\ref{sf:CE2} as printed, whose constants are
independent of $h$, at a cutoff of $\CE$, in the following orientations (\S\ref{sec:filter}
proves a Hamiltonian clustering lemma of its own, Lemma~\ref{lem:cluster}).  In
Lemma~\ref{lem:ce2strip} and in Theorem~\ref{thm:D3} (hence in \S\ref{sec:G}) the strips are
parallel to the time-zero line (for Lemma~\ref{lem:ce2strip}, with two sharp-time monomials, the
case the printed proof reaches; for Theorem~\ref{thm:D3}, with a time-integrated partner, see
Remark~\ref{aux:d3-orient}); in Theorem~\ref{thm:D4} (hence in
Theorem~\ref{thm:M2} and in everything that consumes $M$) the strips are parallel for the
squares of class (P) and transverse for the squares of class (T), the latter being
A8(iv)-T.  Lemma~\ref{lem:Z}, Lemma~\ref{lem:uv} and Theorem~\ref{thm:stability} supply
the normalization, the ultraviolet limit and the integrability that
Appendix~\ref{app:comparison} consumes.  Hence no cutoff-uniformity assertion beyond the
printed Theorem 1.1.7 is consumed by the proof.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The moment bounds of Theorem~\ref{thm:M2} (Step~5) and Lemmas~\ref{lem:transfer}--%
\ref{lem:twopoint} are Theorems~\ref{thm:D4} and~\ref{thm:D3}, whose proofs apply Source
Fact~\ref{sf:CE2} through Lemma~\ref{lem:D2} at the cutoffs $h_\alpha=h^B+\alpha(h-h^B)\in
\CE$ (Lemma~\ref{aux:notation-cutoffs}(ii)), with the strips named in their Steps~3
(Lemma~\ref{aux:d3-geom}: parallel; the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:D4}, Step~3: classes (P)
and (T)); if $g$ is replaced by an affine path cutoff $g_s$, Lemma~\ref{lem:classes} gives
$g_s\in\CH$, so $h_{T,g_s}\in\CE$.  Lemma~\ref{lem:ce2strip} is the one direct CE2
application, across the parallel strip $\{|x^0|<t/2-1\}$, and Source Fact~\ref{sf:CE2}
quantifies over its cutoff directly.  The remaining assertions are exactly
Lemma~\ref{lem:Z}, Lemma~\ref{lem:uv} and Theorem~\ref{thm:stability}, consumed in
Appendix~\ref{app:comparison} as listed in Remark~\ref{aux:d3-V3}.
\end{proof}


% ==================================================================
\section{The uniform local moment from the printed clustering theorem}\label{app:comparison}
\renewcommand{\thesubsection}{\thesection.\alph{subsection}}
\renewcommand{\thesubsubsection}{\thesubsection.\arabic{subsubsection}}
% Appendix D, integrated 7 November 2026 from ugjs/comparison.tex (the comparison note of
% Phases A and B of ugjs/PLAN.md, drafted 31 October-4 November 2026 by the council members aqft,
% mp and cqft2, audited in the rounds V1, V2, V1b, V2b and V1c recorded in ugjs/council/).  The
% auxiliary statements keep the numbers N.k of the note; the one addition is Lemma N.23' (the
% identification of the constructed Wick powers with those of Assumptions A1 and A3, which closes
% the gap the note recorded after Lemma N.20).  The note's sections 1 (purpose), 8 (the replaced
% proofs, applied in sections 4-7) and Appendices A.5-A.6 and B (audit records) are not part of
% this appendix; ugjs/comparison.tex stays in the repository as the audited pre-integration text.

\emph{Purpose.}  Theorem~\ref{thm:M2} and Lemmas~\ref{lem:transfer}--\ref{lem:twopoint}
and~\ref{lem:expmom} consume moment bounds for sharp-time observables under the interacting
Euclidean measures $dq_h$ at slab cutoffs $h=h_{T,g}$, uniformly in $T>0$.  Until 7 November
2026 these were supplied by Assumption~\ref{A:gjs}(vi), read off GJS Theorem~1.1.11 at $B=I$,
$n=0$.  This appendix derives them from the printed clustering theorem, GJS Theorem~1.1.7,
(1.1.10) \cite[p.~594]{GJS}, imported as Assumption~\ref{A:gjs}(iv), together with
Lemma~\ref{lem:Z}, Lemma~\ref{lem:uv} and Theorem~\ref{thm:stability} of
Appendix~\ref{app:huniform} and the imports A2, A3, A4 and A8(i)--(iii).  The device is the
source's own (p.~595): compare $dq_h$ with the measure of the cutoff restricted to a box of
GJS squares around the observable; the squares outside the box are strip-separated from it,
so that (1.1.10) bounds their terms, and the box term is bounded by Lemma~\ref{lem:Z} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:stability}.  Theorem~\ref{thm:D3} (Phase~A of \texttt{ugjs/PLAN.md}) gives
the bound at fixed spatial cutoff $g$, with constants depending on $g$, which is all
\S\ref{sec:G} needs, across strips parallel to the time-zero line only; Theorem~\ref{thm:D4}
(Phase~B) gives the bound of Theorem~\ref{thm:M2} uniformly in the cutoff, across transverse
strips as well.  Lemmas~\ref{lem:D1} and~\ref{lem:D2} serve both.

\emph{Standard.}  A printed statement is imported at the scope at which it is printed; this
is the standard under which Assumptions~\ref{A:cutoff}--\ref{A:gjs} are imported, and this
appendix uses no other.  None of GJS's proofs is verified here.  A case of Theorem~1.1.7 is
used that its printed proof does not reach: GJS Theorem 1.1.7 is printed for localized
monomials separated by a strip of any orientation (p.~594). Its printed proof rotates the
strip into the time direction (p.~597) and so reaches sharp-time monomials only across strips
parallel to the time-zero line. Lemma~\ref{lem:ce2strip} uses only that case; this appendix
also uses the transverse case, listed separately as A8(iv)-T (Assumption~\ref{A:gjs}, item
(iv-T)).  Theorem~\ref{thm:D4} uses it, and every such use is flagged where it occurs;
Theorem~\ref{thm:D3} uses the parallel case only.

\emph{What the appendix does not do.}  It does not prove the transverse case of
Theorem~1.1.7; it does not re-derive the cluster expansion; it does not evaluate the
constant of Theorem~\ref{thm:stability}, which enters the constants below as $S_2(B)$; and
it proves nothing about the net of ground states beyond what \S\S\ref{sec:M2}--\ref{sec:G}
derive from its two theorems.  The open human readings it rests on, items 1--4, 9(c) and 10--12 of
\texttt{part\_i/C5\_reading\_record.md}, are named beside every status word.

\emph{Status and provenance.}  Lemmas~\ref{lem:D1}--\ref{lem:D2} and
Theorems~\ref{thm:D3}--\ref{thm:D4} are proved from Assumptions~\ref{A:fkn},
\ref{A:stability}, \ref{A:hyper} and \ref{A:gjs}(i)--(iv) (for Theorem~\ref{thm:D4} including
A8(iv)-T), from Lemma~\ref{lem:Z}, Lemma~\ref{lem:uv} and Theorem~\ref{thm:stability} as
repaired on 7 November 2026, and from the auxiliary statements of this appendix, under the
reading of Remark~\ref{aux:notation-gjslattice}(iv) (that the objects constructed here are
the monomials of p.~593 and the $\mathcal U_h$ of Assumption~\ref{A:stability} is GJS's
$V(h)$), which Assumption~\ref{A:gjs}, Scope~(d), records.  The text was drafted as the
comparison note \texttt{ugjs/comparison.tex} (31 October--4 November 2026) by three council
members and audited in four rounds (\texttt{ugjs/council/}: V1 by an attacker and a
quantifier checker, V2 by a fresh auditor, re-checks V1b/V2b, closing pass V1c).  At integration the conditions those audits attached to every statement (repairs
R1--R10 of the V3 audit of Appendix~\ref{app:huniform}; the gap recorded after
Lemma~\ref{aux:notation-wick}) were discharged by the repairs of Appendix~\ref{app:huniform},
the Wick-power paragraphs of Assumptions~\ref{A:cutoff} and~\ref{A:stability}, and the new
Lemma~\ref{aux:notation-ident}; the integration gate is recorded in \texttt{part\_i/README.md}.
References to ``the plan'', and to its traps, findings, rulings and gates, are to
\texttt{ugjs/PLAN.md}.  The numbering N.$k$ of the note's auxiliary statements is kept, so that the
audit reports apply to this text; the note's Lemma N.47 (a Gaussian moment inequality of its
\S8, which is Lemma~\ref{aux:constants-gauss} here) is omitted, and Lemma~N.23$'$ is new.

\subsection{Notation and standing conventions}\label{sec:notation}
% notation.tex -- drafted by cqft2 (council of ugjs/PLAN.md), 2 November 2026.
% Read for this part: part_i/main.tex (state of 2 November 2026; numbers and pages from
% labels-main.txt, re-checked against main.aux the same day); GJS pp. 589, 593, 594 on the
% page images gjs74/p-06, p-10, p-11 (p. 594 also at 300 dpi).  No other source is used.
% Integrated 7 November 2026: references to the record are \ref's; the V3-conditional status
% wording is replaced by the integrated status (see the subsection "Status and provenance").

This section fixes the notation of \texttt{ugjs/PLAN.md}, Part~1, and proves the elementary
facts that the later sections use: that every cutoff of the comparison lies in $\CE$
(Lemma~\ref{aux:notation-cutoffs}), the strip geometry (Lemma~\ref{aux:notation-strip}), the
ring count around the $3\times5$ box (Lemma~\ref{aux:notation-rings}), the finiteness and the
translation invariance of the stability constant $S_2$
(Proposition~\ref{aux:notation-S2}), the norm of the interaction in one square
(Lemma~\ref{aux:notation-cU}), the Wick isometry with the constant $V_*$ and the comparison
$\|Y\|_4\le3^{p'}\|Y\|_2$ (Lemmas~\ref{aux:notation-wick} and \ref{aux:notation-hyper}), the
identification of the constructed Wick powers with those of Assumptions~\ref{A:cutoff}
and~\ref{A:stability} (Lemma~\ref{aux:notation-ident}), and the list of printed constants
(\S\ref{sec:notation-printed}).

\emph{Appendix~\ref{app:huniform}.}  Lemma~\ref{lem:Z} (normalization), Lemma~\ref{lem:uv}
(ultraviolet difference) and Theorem~\ref{thm:stability} (stability) are load-bearing in this
appendix: Lemma~\ref{lem:uv} in Lemma~\ref{aux:notation-cutoffs}(iv),
Lemma~\ref{aux:notation-transl}(iii)--(iv) and Lemma~\ref{aux:notation-cU}(iii)--(iv), and
through them in Proposition~\ref{aux:notation-S2}(iii); Theorem~\ref{thm:stability} in
Proposition~\ref{aux:notation-S2}(i); the moment comparison of its proof (Theorem I.22 of
Simon's book, Assumption~\ref{A:hyper}) in Lemma~\ref{aux:notation-hyper}(ii).  Their proofs
rest on the constructions of Assumptions~\ref{A:cutoff} and~\ref{A:stability}, on
Assumption~\ref{A:hyper} and on the kernel facts of this section, with every constant
explicit (the V3 audit of 2 November 2026 found them true but resting on three unimported
inputs; the repairs of 7 November 2026 are recorded in Appendix~\ref{app:provenance}).

\emph{Inputs of this section.}  \S\ref{subsec:model}, Definition~\ref{def:classes},
Assumptions~\ref{A:cutoff} (the Wick powers of the time-zero field), \ref{A:fkn},
\ref{A:stability} (the construction of $\mathcal U_h$), \ref{A:hyper} (its abstract form,
in Lemma~\ref{aux:notation-hyper}) and \ref{A:gjs}(i)--(iv), Remark~\ref{rem:conv},
\S\ref{subsec:weak}, Remark~\ref{rem:wlog}, the proofs of Lemma~\ref{lem:cells} and
Proposition~\ref{prop:gjsnorms} (on the columns of the GJS squares,
Remark~\ref{aux:notation-counts}), the regularization of Appendix~\ref{app:huniform} with
Lemma~\ref{lem:uv} and Theorem~\ref{thm:stability}; from GJS, pp.~589, 593 and 594 as quoted.
No other source is used, and no import is made beyond Assumptions~\ref{A:cutoff}--\ref{A:gjs}.

\subsubsection{Coordinates, squares, rows and columns}\label{sec:notation-squares}

\emph{Working coordinates.}  We work in the working coordinates, \S\ref{subsec:weak} and Remark~\ref{rem:wlog}: the bare mass is $m_0=M_*$ and
$\lambda<\lambda_*$, ``so that the \emph{unit-lattice} statements of Assumption~\ref{A:gjs} apply
literally in the working coordinates'' (\S\ref{subsec:weak}).  A point of the Euclidean plane is
$z=(x^0,x^1)\in\R^2$, with $x^0$ the Euclidean time and $x^1$ the space coordinate; the
slab cutoffs are $h_{T,g}=\mathbf 1_{[-T,T]}\otimes g$, i.e.\
$h_{T,g}(z)=\mathbf 1_{[-T,T]}(x^0)\,g(x^1)$ (Assumption~\ref{A:fkn}).  GJS also put
time first: ``$\Phi(\tau,f)\equiv\Phi(\delta_\tau\otimes f)=\int\Phi(\tau,\vec x)f(\vec
x)d\vec x$'' \cite[p.~589]{GJS}.

\emph{The squares of GJS.}  The observables of GJS are the monomials (1.1.5)--(1.1.6), and
p.~593 defines their localization as follows (read on the page image, quoted verbatim,
misprints kept): ``$Q$ is said to be localized if $w(x)$ is supported in a product
$\Delta_{j_1}\times\cdots\times\Delta_{j_n}\subset R^{2r}$ where $\Delta_{j_i}$ is the unit
lattice square in $R^2$ centered at the lattice side $j_i\in Z^2$. We assume a bound on the
$n_i$ in (1.1.5), $n_i<\bar n$. Any choice $\bar n\geqq\deg P$ is sufficient. Since there is
no restriction on $r$, $Q$ still may have an arbitrarily large degree.'' and ``For a
localized monomial $Q$ or $Q(x_0)$, let $N(\Delta)$ be the local degree, i.e.\ the sum of
the $n_i$ for which $\Delta_{j_i}=\Delta$.'' \cite[p.~593]{GJS}.  (``side'' stands for
``site''; the last index of the product is printed $n$ although the product lies in
$R^{2r}$.)  Page~593 prints no convention for points on the lines of the lattice; the
following definition supplies one, and it is used everywhere in this appendix.

\begin{auxdef}[squares, rows, columns; the convention on lattice lines]\label{aux:notation-squares}
For $(l,k)\in\Z^2$ let
\[
 \Delta_{l,k}:=[l-\tfrac12,\,l+\tfrac12)\times[k-\tfrac12,\,k+\tfrac12)\subset\R^2 ,
\]
the first factor being time.  \emph{Row} $l$ is $\bigcup_{k\in\Z}\Delta_{l,k}=[l-\frac12,
l+\frac12)\times\R$ and \emph{column} $k$ is $\bigcup_{l\in\Z}\Delta_{l,k}=\R\times
[k-\frac12,k+\frac12)$.  For a time $s\in\R$,
\[
 l_s:=\lfloor s+\tfrac12\rfloor
\]
is the row containing $s$, i.e.\ the unique integer with $l_s-\frac12\le s<l_s+\frac12$;
for $x^1\in\R$, $k(x^1):=\lfloor x^1+\frac12\rfloor$ is the column containing $x^1$.  In
particular a time on a lattice line, $s=l-\frac12$ with $l\in\Z$, lies in row $l_s=l$.  The
\emph{sup-index distance} of $\Delta_{l,k}$ and $\Delta_{l',k'}$ is
$\max\{|l-l'|,|k-k'|\}$.
\end{auxdef}

\begin{auxrem}[relation to the squares of p.~593 and to the former cells]\label{aux:notation-gjslattice}
\emph{(i)} The squares $\Delta_{l,k}$ partition $\R^2$: the point $z$ lies in exactly one of
them, $\Delta_{l_{x^0},k(x^1)}$.  The closure
$\overline{\Delta}_{l,k}=[l-\frac12,l+\frac12]\times[k-\frac12,k+\frac12]$ is the unit
lattice square centred at the lattice site $(l,k)\in\Z^2$ of p.~593.  A kernel that vanishes
almost everywhere outside a product of squares $\Delta_{j_1}\times\cdots\times\Delta_{j_r}$
has its (closed) support in the product of their closures, so the monomial is localized in
the sense of p.~593; this is how ``localized'' is read in this appendix, for (1.1.5) and for
(1.1.6) alike (Assumption~\ref{A:gjs}(ii) defines ``localized'' for both forms).

\emph{(ii)} For a sharp-time factor at time $x_{i,0}$, localization in a closed square
requires $x_{i,0}\in[l-\frac12,l+\frac12]$; a time on a lattice line lies in two closed
squares of the same column, and Definition~\ref{aux:notation-squares} assigns the factor to
the square of row $l_{x_{i,0}}$.  Thus a sharp-time monomial at time $s$ whose spatial
kernel vanishes almost everywhere outside $\prod_i[k_i-\frac12,k_i+\frac12)$ is localized in
the squares $\Delta_{l_s,k_i}$, and these are \emph{its localization squares} in this appendix.
The choice of row changes neither the local degrees nor $\|w(x_0,\cdot)\|_2$, hence not the
norm (1.1.8) (\eqref{eq:printednorm}; cf.\ Remark~\ref{rem:row}); the
strips of \S\ref{sec:notation-strips} are computed for the row $l_s$, and every strip
written there for that assignment separates the closed squares as well.

\emph{(iii)} Until 7 November 2026, \S\S\ref{sec:M2}--\ref{sec:norms} counted in the cells
$[j,j+1)$, ``this lattice shifted by one half unit in space'' (the former wording of
Assumption~\ref{A:gjs}(ii)); since then they count, as this appendix does, on the columns of
the squares of Definition~\ref{aux:notation-squares} (finding F3 of \texttt{ugjs/PLAN.md}).
The next remark records the one place where the two lattices give different counts.

\emph{(iv) A reading of the source.}  GJS print (1.1.5)--(1.1.6) as formal integrals (p.~593),
define the action ``(1.8) $V(h)=\lambda\int_{R^2}:\mathscr P(\Phi(x)):h(x)dx$ as a function on
$\mathscr S'(R^2)$, where $:\ :$ denotes Wick order with respect to the measure $d\Phi$, as in
[9], [1]'' (p.~589), and give no construction.  That the random variables of this appendix --- the
one-square interactions $U_{\Delta,n}$ (Definition~\ref{aux:notation-UDelta}), the sharp-time
objects of Lemmas~\ref{aux:notation-wick}--\ref{aux:notation-sharpid}, the regularized limits of
\S\ref{sec:d1} and the pieces of $Q_V$ in \S\ref{sec:d4} --- are GJS's monomials of p.~593 under
GJS's $dq_h$, and that the $\Uc_h$ of Assumption~\ref{A:stability} is GJS's $V(h)$, is a
\emph{reading} of the source, of the kind Assumption~\ref{A:gjs}, Scope~(d), names and
records.  It is named in the status lines of Lemma~\ref{lem:D2} and
Theorems~\ref{thm:D3}--\ref{thm:D4}.  Item~10 of \texttt{part\_i/C5\_reading\_record.md}, still open, asks a
human reader to confirm the printed text of (1.1.5)--(1.1.6) and of the multi-time notation
(pp.~593, 595) on which the reading rests; it does not check the reading itself.  At sharp time,
Lemma~\ref{aux:notation-sharpid} shows that the two regularizations used in this appendix give the
same objects.
\end{auxrem}

\begin{auxrem}[counts on the squares of p.~593]\label{aux:notation-counts}
For a bounded interval $I\subset\R$ of length $L$ put
\[
 N^{\square}_I:=\#\{k\in\Z:\ |I\cap[k-\tfrac12,k+\tfrac12)|>0\},
\]
the number of columns meeting $I$ in positive measure, and let
$N^{\rm cell}_I:=\#\{j\in\Z:\ |I\cap[j,j+1)|>0\}$ be the count on the cells $[j,j+1)$ that
\S\ref{sec:norms} used until 7 November 2026 (since then $N_I=N^\square_I$ there).

\emph{(i)} $N^{\square}_I\le\lfloor L\rfloor+2$.  The proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:cells}
uses only that the cells are consecutive unit intervals partitioning $\R$; run with the
columns: if $k_{\min}\le k_{\max}$ are the extreme columns meeting $I$ in positive measure,
then $\inf I<k_{\min}+\frac12$ and $\sup I>k_{\max}-\frac12$, so
$L>k_{\max}-k_{\min}-1$, whence $k_{\max}-k_{\min}-1\le\lceil L\rceil-1\le\lfloor
L\rfloor$ and $N^{\square}_I\le k_{\max}-k_{\min}+1\le\lfloor L\rfloor+2$.

\emph{(ii)} The two counts differ in general: $I=(-0.1,0.1)$ gives $N^{\rm cell}_I=2$ and
$N^{\square}_I=1$; $I=(0.4,0.6)$ gives $N^{\rm cell}_I=1$ and $N^{\square}_I=2$.

\emph{(iii)} Consequently the bounds of Proposition~\ref{prop:gjsnorms},
\eqref{eq:normQ}--\eqref{eq:normQ2}, and the constant $K'=K_2^2N_I^{1/2}\|f\|_2$ of
Lemma~\ref{lem:expmom} are stated, for norms computed on the squares of
Definition~\ref{aux:notation-squares}, with the column count $N_I=N^{\square}_I$ (as they are
since 7 November 2026; until then they carried $N^{\rm cell}_I$).  The proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:gjsnorms} decomposes $f_i=\sum_kf_i\mathbf 1_{[k-\frac12,k+\frac12)}$ over
the columns and applies the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality over the $N_I=N^\square_I$ columns
(with the former cells $[j,j+1)$ the same argument gives bounds with $N^{\rm cell}_I$, but for
pieces localized in cells, which are not the localization squares of the norm of p.~594); it
gives
\[
 \GJS{Q}\le K_1K_2^{2r}r!\,(N^{\square}_I)^{r/2}\prod_{i=1}^r\|f_i\|_2 ,\qquad
 \GJS{Q^2}\le K_1K_2^{4r}(2r)!\,(N^{\square}_I)^{r}\prod_{i=1}^r\|f_i\|_2^2
 \tag{N.a}
\]
for a cylinder monomial $Q=\prod_{i=1}^r\varphi_0(f_i)$ at one sharp time, $\supp
f_i\subseteq I$.  With the cell count $N^{\rm cell}_I$ the first bound can fail on these squares: for $r=1$ and
$f$ supported in $(0.4,0.6)$ and even about $\frac12$, the localized pieces of
$Q=\varphi_0(f)$ on the squares of Definition~\ref{aux:notation-squares} are
$\varphi_0(f\mathbf 1_{[-1/2,1/2)})$ and $\varphi_0(f\mathbf 1_{[1/2,3/2)})$, and (1.1.9)
gives $\GJS{Q}=K_1K_2^2(\|f\mathbf 1_{[-1/2,1/2)}\|_2+\|f\mathbf
1_{[1/2,3/2)}\|_2)=\sqrt2\,K_1K_2^2\|f\|_2$, while \eqref{eq:normQ} with $N^{\rm cell}_I=1$
would assert $\GJS{Q}\le K_1K_2^2\|f\|_2$ (this is the finding recorded in
Appendix~\ref{app:provenance}, repaired by counting columns).  Both counts obey the bound
(i), so every constant written with $\lfloor L\rfloor+2$ in place of the count is valid on
both lattices.
\end{auxrem}

\subsubsection{Boxes, the restricted cutoff $h^B$ and the path $h_\alpha$}\label{sec:notation-boxes}

\begin{auxdef}[boxes; restriction; charged squares]\label{aux:notation-box}
A \emph{box} is a set
\[
 B=\bigcup_{l=l_1}^{l_2}\ \bigcup_{k=k_1}^{k_2}\Delta_{l,k}
 =[l_1-\tfrac12,\,l_2+\tfrac12)\times[k_1-\tfrac12,\,k_2+\tfrac12),
 \qquad l_1\le l_2,\ k_1\le k_2\ \text{integers};
\]
its \emph{shape} is the pair $(l_2-l_1+1,\;k_2-k_1+1)$ of the numbers of its rows and
columns, and $|B|:=(l_2-l_1+1)(k_2-k_1+1)$ is the number of its squares, which equals the
area of $B$ and of its closure $\overline B$.  Since the squares partition $\R^2$ and $B$ is
a union of squares, a square $\Delta$ satisfies $\Delta\not\subseteq B$ if and only if
$\Delta\cap B=\varnothing$.  For a function $h$ on $\R^2$ and a box $B$,
\[
 h^B:=h\,\mathbf 1_B,\qquad h_\alpha:=h^B+\alpha\,(h-h^B)\quad(\alpha\in[0,1]),
\]
and $\mathfrak S(h):=\{\Delta:\int_\Delta h\,dz>0\}$ is the set of squares \emph{charged}
by $h$.  For a measurable $h$, $\supp h$ denotes the closure of $\{z:h(z)\ne0\}$.
\end{auxdef}

The class $\CE$ is Definition~\ref{def:classes}:
\[
 \text{``}\CE:=\{h:\R^2\to[0,1]\ \text{measurable},\ \supp h\ \text{compact}\}
 \quad\text{(Euclidean class)''},
\]
``the printed class[\,] of \cite[p.~589]{GJS}''; on p.~589 GJS write ``Let $h$ be
a function with compact support in $R^2$, and let $0\leqq h\leqq1$.''  Assumption~\ref{A:gjs}(i) reads: ``For $h\in\CE$, $dq_h=Z(h)^{-1}e^{-\mathcal U_h}d\mu_0$ as in
Assumption~\ref{A:stability}''; and Assumption~\ref{A:stability} gives, for every $h\in\CE$,
$\Uc_h\in L^p(\dmu)$ for every $p<\infty$ and $0<Z(h)<\infty$.

\begin{auxlemma}[the cutoffs of the comparison lie in $\CE$]\label{aux:notation-cutoffs}
Let $h\in\CE$ and let $B$ be a box.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[\textup{(i)}] $h^B$, $h-h^B$ and $h\mathbf 1_\Delta$ (for every square $\Delta$) belong
to $\CE$, and $\supp h^B\subseteq\overline B\cap\supp h$.
\item[\textup{(ii)}] For every $\alpha\in[0,1]$, $h_\alpha=(1-\alpha)h^B+\alpha h=h\,(\mathbf
1_B+\alpha\mathbf 1_{\R^2\setminus B})$ belongs to $\CE$; moreover $h^B\le h_\alpha\le h$
pointwise, $h_0=h^B$, $h_1=h$, and $\supp h_\alpha\subseteq\supp h$.
\item[\textup{(iii)}] $\mathfrak S(h)$ is finite, every $\Delta\in\mathfrak S(h)$ meets
$\supp h$, and $h-h^B=\sum_{\Delta\in\mathfrak S(h),\ \Delta\cap B=\varnothing}h\mathbf
1_\Delta$ almost everywhere.
\item[\textup{(iv)}] If $h_1,\dots,h_m\in\CE$ and $c_1,\dots,c_m\in\R$ satisfy
$\sum_ic_ih_i=0$ almost everywhere, then $\sum_ic_i\Uc_{h_i}=0$ in $L^2(\dmu)$.  In
particular
\[
 \Uc_{h_\alpha}=\Uc_{h^B}+\alpha\,\Uc_{h-h^B}\quad(\alpha\in[0,1]),\qquad
 \Uc_{h-h^B}=\sum_{\Delta\in\mathfrak S(h),\ \Delta\cap B=\varnothing}\Uc_{h\mathbf 1_\Delta} .
\]
\end{enumerate}
\end{auxlemma}

\begin{proof}
(i) $B$ and $\Delta$ are Borel sets, so $h\mathbf 1_B$, $h\mathbf 1_{\R^2\setminus B}=h-h^B$
and $h\mathbf 1_\Delta$ are products of measurable functions, hence measurable; they take
values in $[0,1]$ because $h$ does and the indicators take values in $\{0,1\}$.  Each
vanishes wherever $h$ does, so its set of non-zeros lies in $\{h\ne0\}$ and its support is
a closed subset of the compact set $\supp h$, hence compact.  For $h^B$ the non-zeros lie in
$B\cap\{h\ne0\}$, whose closure lies in $\overline B\cap\supp h$.

(ii) Pointwise, $h^B+\alpha(h-h^B)=h\mathbf 1_B+\alpha h\mathbf 1_{\R^2\setminus B}$, which
equals $(1-\alpha)h^B+\alpha h$.  For $0\le\alpha\le1$ and $0\le h\le1$ this gives $0\le
h\mathbf 1_B\le h_\alpha\le h\le1$; $h_\alpha$ is measurable, and $\{h_\alpha\ne0\}\subseteq
\{h\ne0\}$ gives $\supp h_\alpha\subseteq\supp h$, compact.  At $\alpha=0,1$ the formula
gives $h^B$ and $h$.

(iii) $\supp h$ is compact, hence contained in $[-\rho,\rho]^2$ for some $\rho$, which meets
only finitely many squares.  If $\int_\Delta h>0$, the set $\Delta\cap\{h>0\}$ has positive
measure, so $\Delta$ meets $\{h\ne0\}\subseteq\supp h$; hence $\mathfrak S(h)$ is finite.
Since the squares partition $\R^2$ and $B$ is a union of squares, $\mathbf 1_{\R^2\setminus
B}=\sum_{\Delta\cap B=\varnothing}\mathbf 1_\Delta$ pointwise, so $h-h^B=\sum_{\Delta\cap
B=\varnothing}h\mathbf 1_\Delta$ pointwise, with only the finitely many squares meeting
$\{h\ne0\}$ contributing.  If $\Delta\notin\mathfrak S(h)$, then $h\ge0$ and $\int_\Delta
h=0$ give $h\mathbf 1_\Delta=0$ almost everywhere, and these terms may be dropped.

(iv) We use the regularization of Appendix~\ref{app:huniform} (the notation paragraph before Lemma~\ref{lem:Z}): $\Phi_\kappa=\delta_\kappa*\Phi$ with an even mollifier
$\delta_\kappa$, the variance $c_\kappa$ of $\Phi_\kappa(z)$, and
$\Uc_{h,\kappa}=\lambda\int h\,{:}\mathscr P(\Phi_\kappa){:}_{c_\kappa}dz$.  For every
$q\in\mathcal S'(\R^2)$ the function $z\mapsto\Phi_\kappa(z)(q)=(q*\delta_\kappa)(z)$ is
smooth, and ${:}\mathscr P(y){:}_{c}=\sum_na_nc^{n/2}\operatorname{He}_n(y/\sqrt c)$ is a
polynomial in $y$; so $\Uc_{h,\kappa}(q)$ is the integral of $h$ against a function bounded
on the compact $\supp h$, linear in $h$ and unchanged when $h$ is changed on a null set.
Hence $\sum_ic_i\Uc_{h_i,\kappa}=\lambda\int(\sum_ic_ih_i){:}\mathscr
P(\Phi_\kappa){:}_{c_\kappa}dz=0$ pointwise.  Lemma~\ref{lem:uv} states: for all $h\in\CE$ with
$\supp h\subseteq\Lambda$ and all $\kappa\ge2$, $\|\Uc_h-\Uc_{h,\kappa}\|_{L^2(d\mu_0)}^2\le
c_2\,\kappa^{-1}(\log\kappa)^{2p'}$, with the explicit $c_2=c_2(\lambda,\mathscr P,\Lambda)$
given there.  Its hypotheses hold for each $h_i$, with $\Lambda:=\bigcup_i\supp h_i$
(compact) and every $\kappa\ge2$; $\Uc_h$ is the element of $L^2(\dmu)$ constructed in
Assumption~\ref{A:stability}.  Therefore
\[
 \Bigl\|\sum_ic_i\Uc_{h_i}\Bigr\|_{L^2(\dmu)}
 \le\sum_i|c_i|\,\|\Uc_{h_i}-\Uc_{h_i,\kappa}\|_{L^2(\dmu)}
 \le\Bigl(\sum_i|c_i|\Bigr)\bigl(c_2\kappa^{-1}(\log\kappa)^{2p'}\bigr)^{1/2}
 \xrightarrow[\kappa\to\infty]{}0 .
\]
The two displayed identities are the cases $h_\alpha-h^B-\alpha(h-h^B)=0$ (all three in
$\CE$ by (i)--(ii)) and (iii).  The same identities also follow directly from the linearity
of $h\mapsto{:}\Phi^n(h){:}$ in the construction of Assumption~\ref{A:stability}, and
without Lemma~\ref{lem:uv} from Step~1 of the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:D1} (\S\ref{sec:d1}),
which proves from Lemma~\ref{aux:d1-moments}(a) that the regularizations converge in
$L^2(\dmu)$ for every bounded kernel of compact support and that the limit is linear in the
kernel; that this limit is the $\Uc_h$ of Assumption~\ref{A:stability} is
Lemma~\ref{aux:notation-ident}(ii) (until 7 November 2026 the manuscript stated no
construction of $\Uc_h$, and the identification was a condition of this appendix).
\end{proof}

In Lemma~\ref{lem:D1} the path is used with $h_1=h^B$ and $h_2=h$; there
$h_2-h_1=h-h^B\ge0$, and it enters only as the observable $\Uc_{h-h^B}$, never as a cutoff.

\subsubsection{Strips and the strip width}\label{sec:notation-strips}

The exponent of (1.1.10) is a strip width, printed on p.~594 as follows (read on the page
image, quoted verbatim; the sentence ends without a period in the source): ``For two
localized monomials $Q_1$ and $Q_2$, with disjoint localization squares
$\{\Delta^{(1)}_{j_i}\}$ and $\{\Delta^{(2)}_{j_l}\}$ respectively, let $d$ be the width of
the widest strip bounded by two parallel lines, and separating the $\{\Delta^{(1)}_{j_i}\}$
from the $\{\Delta^{(2)}_{j_l}\}$'' \cite[p.~594]{GJS}.  No orientation is prescribed.

\begin{auxdef}[strips; separation; orientation; strip width]\label{aux:notation-stripdef}
Let $L_1,L_2$ be parallel lines in $\R^2$.  If $L_1\ne L_2$, the open region between them is
a \emph{strip} of width $\dist(L_1,L_2)$, and its complement is the union of two disjoint
closed half-planes $H_1,H_2$ with boundaries $L_1,L_2$.  If $L_1=L_2=:L$, the strip is the
line $L$ itself, of width $0$, and $H_1,H_2$ are the two closed half-planes bounded by $L$.
The strip \emph{separates} $X\subseteq\R^2$ from $Y\subseteq\R^2$ if $X\subseteq H_1$ and
$Y\subseteq H_2$, or the other way round; since $H_1,H_2$ are closed, it then also separates
$\overline X$ from $\overline Y$.  The strip is \emph{parallel} (to the time-zero line
$\{x^0=0\}$) if $L_1,L_2$ are lines $\{x^0=\text{const}\}$, and \emph{transverse}
otherwise.  For a square $\Delta$ and a non-empty finite set $R$ of squares with
$\Delta\notin R$,
\[
 d(\Delta,R):=\sup\Bigl\{\text{width of }S:\ S\text{ a strip separating }\Delta\text{ from }
 \textstyle\bigcup_{\Delta'\in R}\Delta'\Bigr\},
\]
defined whenever some strip separates them.
\end{auxdef}

If $Q_2$ is localized in $\Delta$ and $Q_1$ in squares of $R$, then every strip separating
$\Delta$ from $\bigcup R$ separates the localization squares of $Q_2$ from those of $Q_1$, so
its width $w$ is at most the $d$ of p.~594, and $e^{-m_1d}\le e^{-m_1w}$ because $m_1>0$.
Every application of (1.1.10) in this appendix is made in this weakened form, with the width of
one explicit separating strip.

\begin{auxlemma}[strip width]\label{aux:notation-strip}
Let $S:=\{\Delta_{l',k'}:l_1\le l'\le l_2,\ k_1\le k'\le k_2\}$ be a rectangle of squares, let
$R\subseteq S$ be non-empty, and let $\Delta=\Delta_{l,k}\notin S$.  Put
\[
 \rho_t:=\max\{l_1-l,\,0,\,l-l_2\},\qquad\rho_x:=\max\{k_1-k,\,0,\,k-k_2\},\qquad
 m:=\max\{\rho_t,\rho_x\},
\]
so that $m\ge1$ is the sup-index distance from $\Delta$ to the nearest square of $S$.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[\textup{(i)}] If $\rho_t\ge1$, the strip bounded by the lines $x^0=l_2+\frac12$ and
$x^0=l-\frac12$ (if $l>l_2$), resp.\ $x^0=l+\frac12$ and $x^0=l_1-\frac12$ (if $l<l_1$), is
\emph{parallel}, has width $\rho_t-1$, and separates $\Delta$ from $\bigcup S$.
\item[\textup{(ii)}] If $\rho_x\ge1$, the strip bounded by the lines $x^1=k_2+\frac12$ and
$x^1=k-\frac12$ (if $k>k_2$), resp.\ $x^1=k+\frac12$ and $x^1=k_1-\frac12$ (if $k<k_1$), is
\emph{transverse} (it is perpendicular to the time-zero line), has width $\rho_x-1$, and
separates $\Delta$ from $\bigcup S$.
\item[\textup{(iii)}] $d(\Delta,R)$ is defined and $d(\Delta,R)\ge m-1$.  If $m\ge2$, the
strip of \textup{(i)} or \textup{(ii)} realising $m-1$ has width $\ge1$.
\item[\textup{(iv)}] \textup{(Rows next to $R$.)}  If $R$ contains a square in a row $l'$
with $|l-l'|\le1$, then no parallel strip of positive width separates $\Delta$ from
$\bigcup R$: every strip of positive width that separates them is transverse.
\item[\textup{(v)}] \textup{(Monotonicity.)}  If $\varnothing\ne R_1\subseteq R$, then
$d(\Delta,R_1)\ge d(\Delta,R)$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{auxlemma}

\begin{proof}
Every square $\Delta_{l',k'}$ is contained in $\{l'-\frac12\le x^0<l'+\frac12\}$ and in
$\{k'-\frac12\le x^1<k'+\frac12\}$.

(i) Let $l>l_2$, so $\rho_t=l-l_2\ge1$.  Then $\bigcup S\subseteq\{x^0\le l_2+\frac12\}$ and
$\Delta\subseteq\{x^0\ge l-\frac12\}$.  If $\rho_t\ge2$, the lines $x^0=l_2+\frac12$ and
$x^0=l-\frac12$ are distinct, the open strip $\{l_2+\frac12<x^0<l-\frac12\}$ has width
$(l-\frac12)-(l_2+\frac12)=\rho_t-1$, and the two closed half-planes just named are the
complement of that strip; if $\rho_t=1$ the two lines coincide and the strip of width $0$
separates.  The case $l<l_1$ is the same with $\bigcup S\subseteq\{x^0\ge l_1-\frac12\}$,
$\Delta\subseteq\{x^0\le l+\frac12\}$ and width $(l_1-\frac12)-(l+\frac12)=l_1-l-1=\rho_t-1$.
The bounding lines are lines of constant $x^0$, so the strip is parallel.

(ii) The same computation in the coordinate $x^1$, with columns in place of rows; the
bounding lines $\{x^1=\text{const}\}$ are perpendicular to $\{x^0=0\}$, so the strip is
transverse.

(iii) $\Delta\notin S$ means $l\notin[l_1,l_2]$ or $k\notin[k_1,k_2]$, i.e.\ $m\ge1$.  If
$m=\rho_t$, (i) gives a strip of width $m-1$ separating $\Delta$ from $\bigcup S$;
otherwise $m=\rho_x$ and (ii) does.  As $\bigcup R\subseteq\bigcup S$, the same strip
separates $\Delta$ from $\bigcup R$.  So $d(\Delta,R)$ is defined and at least $m-1$, which
is $\ge1$ when $m\ge2$.

(iv) Let $\Delta'\in R$ lie in row $l'$ with $|l-l'|\le1$, and suppose that the parallel
strip $\{\alpha<x^0<\beta\}$, $\beta>\alpha$, separates $\Delta$ from $\bigcup R$.  If
$\Delta\subseteq\{x^0\le\alpha\}$ and $\Delta'\subseteq\{x^0\ge\beta\}$, then, because
$\Delta$ contains points with $x^0$ arbitrarily close to $l+\frac12$ and $\Delta'$ contains
points with $x^0=l'-\frac12$, we get $l+\frac12\le\alpha$ and $l'-\frac12\ge\beta$, whence
$l'-l\ge1+(\beta-\alpha)>1$, a contradiction.  The other case,
$\Delta\subseteq\{x^0\ge\beta\}$ and $\Delta'\subseteq\{x^0\le\alpha\}$, gives
$l-l'>1$ in the same way.

(v) A strip separating $\Delta$ from $\bigcup R$ separates $\Delta$ from the subset
$\bigcup R_1$, so the supremum defining $d(\Delta,R_1)$ is over a larger set.
\end{proof}

\begin{auxrem}[the bound fails for a general $R$]\label{aux:notation-counterex}
The rectangle $S\supseteq R$ in Lemma~\ref{aux:notation-strip} cannot be dropped.  Let
$R=\{\Delta_{0,0},\Delta_{0,4}\}$ and $\Delta=\Delta_{0,2}$ (verifier, item m2 of the R3
report).  Each square of $R$ is at sup-index distance $2$ from $\Delta$, but no strip, of
positive or of zero width, separates $\Delta$ from $\Delta_{0,0}\cup\Delta_{0,4}$: a closed
half-plane containing $\Delta_{0,0}\cup\Delta_{0,4}$ contains the closures and their convex
hull $[-\frac12,\frac12]\times[-\frac12,\frac92]$, which contains
$\overline\Delta_{0,2}=[-\frac12,\frac12]\times[\frac32,\frac52]$; so a separating strip would
place $\Delta$ in $H_1\cap H_2$, which is empty for positive width and a line for width
$0$, and a square lies in neither.  Thus $d(\Delta,R)$ is undefined, (1.1.10) does not apply
to a pair localized in $R$ and in $\Delta$, and the inequality
``$d(\Delta,R)\ge(\text{sup-index distance to the nearest square of }R)-1$'' is false.
Lemma~\ref{aux:notation-strip} measures the distance to a rectangle containing $R$; here
every such rectangle contains $\Delta$.
\end{auxrem}

\begin{auxrem}[tilted strips]\label{aux:notation-tilted}
Lemma~\ref{aux:notation-strip} uses axis-parallel strips and gives lower bounds only; the
widest separating strip may be tilted and wider.  For $R=\{\Delta_{0,0}\}$ and
$\Delta=\Delta_{3,3}$ the axis-parallel strips have width $2$, while the strip between the
lines $x^0+x^1=1$ and $x^0+x^1=5$ separates $\overline\Delta_{0,0}$ (on which $x^0+x^1\le1$)
from $\overline\Delta_{3,3}$ (on which $x^0+x^1\ge5$) and has width $4/\sqrt2=2\sqrt2$.  A
tilted strip is transverse in the sense of Definition~\ref{aux:notation-stripdef}; this appendix
never needs one.
\end{auxrem}

\subsubsection{The $3\times5$ box and its rings}\label{sec:notation-rings}

\begin{auxdef}[the box $B_{3\times5}$ and its rings]\label{aux:notation-ringdef}
For $a\in\R$ let $k_a:=\lfloor a+\frac12\rfloor$ and
\[
 S_a:=\{\Delta_{0,k}:\ k_a\le k\le k_a+2\},
\]
\[
 B^{(j)}:=\bigcup\{\Delta_{l,k}:\ |l|\le1+j,\ k_a-1-j\le k\le k_a+3+j\}\qquad(j\ge0),
\]
and $B_{3\times5}=B_{3\times5}(a):=B^{(0)}$, the rows $-1,0,1$ times the columns
$k_a-1,\dots,k_a+3$.  For $j\ge1$ the \emph{ring} $\mathcal L_j$ is the set of squares
contained in $B^{(j)}$ and not in $B^{(j-1)}$.  For a square $\Delta_{l,k}$ put
$\delta(\Delta_{l,k}):=\max\{|l|-1,\ k_a-1-k,\ k-k_a-3\}$.
\end{auxdef}

\begin{auxlemma}[ring count and strip widths around $B_{3\times5}$]\label{aux:notation-rings}
Let $a\in\R$ and let $R\subseteq S_a$ be non-empty.
\begin{enumerate}
\item[\textup{(i)}] The columns $k$ with $[k-\frac12,k+\frac12)\cap[a,a+2]\ne\varnothing$ are
exactly $k_a,k_a+1,k_a+2$; the columns meeting $[a,a+2]$ in positive measure are among
them.  Hence every square of row $0$ whose column meets $[a,a+2]$ lies in $S_a$, and
$|B_{3\times5}|=15$.
\item[\textup{(ii)}] $\Delta\subseteq B^{(j)}$ if and only if $\delta(\Delta)\le j$.  The rings
$\mathcal L_j=\{\Delta:\delta(\Delta)=j\}$, $j\ge1$, are pairwise disjoint, their union is
the set of all squares $\Delta\not\subseteq B_{3\times5}$, and $|\mathcal L_j|=8j+12$.
\item[\textup{(iii)}] The $20$ squares of $\mathcal L_1$ are $\Delta_{2,k}$ and $\Delta_{-2,k}$
for $k_a-2\le k\le k_a+4$ (fourteen squares) and $\Delta_{l,k_a-2}$, $\Delta_{l,k_a+4}$ for
$|l|\le1$ (six squares).
\item[\textup{(iv)}] Every $\Delta=\Delta_{l,k}\in\mathcal L_j$ satisfies $d(\Delta,R)\ge j$.  If
$|l|=1+j$, a parallel strip of width $j$ separates $\Delta$ from $\bigcup R$.  If $|l|\le j$,
then $\Delta$ lies in column $k_a-1-j$ or $k_a+3+j$, a transverse strip of width $j$
separates it, and every parallel strip separating it has width at most $|l|-1\le j-1$ (none
of positive width if $|l|\le1$).  The four corner squares ($|l|=1+j$ in the two side
columns) admit both strips of width $j$.
\item[\textup{(v)}] For the squares of rows $-1,0,1$ outside $B_{3\times5}$, i.e.\
$\Delta_{l,k}$ with $|l|\le1$ and $k\le k_a-2$ or $k\ge k_a+4$, no parallel strip of positive
width separates $\Delta_{l,k}$ from $\bigcup R$: every strip of positive width separating
them is transverse.
\item[\textup{(vi)}] For every $m_1>0$, with $x:=e^{-m_1}\in(0,1)$, put
\[
 \overline\Sigma_{3\times5}:=\sum_{j\ge1}(8j+12)\,e^{-m_1j}=\frac{4x\,(5-3x)}{(1-x)^2}
 =\frac{4e^{-m_1}\,(5-3e^{-m_1})}{(1-e^{-m_1})^2} .
\]
With the strips of \textup{(iv)}, of width $w_\Delta:=j$ for $\Delta\in\mathcal L_j$,
$\sum_{\Delta\not\subseteq B_{3\times5}}e^{-m_1w_\Delta}=\overline\Sigma_{3\times5}$; and the sum
over the widest strips, which depends on $R$, satisfies
\[
 \Sigma_{3\times5}:=\sum_{\Delta\not\subseteq B_{3\times5}}e^{-m_1d(\Delta,R)}
 \le\overline\Sigma_{3\times5} .
\]
\end{enumerate}
\end{auxlemma}

\begin{proof}
(i) $[k-\frac12,k+\frac12)\cap[a,a+2]\ne\varnothing$ if and only if $k-\frac12\le a+2$ and
$k+\frac12>a$, i.e.\ $a-\frac12<k\le a+\frac52$.  The interval $(a-\frac12,a+\frac52]$ is
half-open of length $3$, so it contains exactly three integers; the smallest integer
$>a-\frac12$ is $\lfloor a-\frac12\rfloor+1=\lfloor a+\frac12\rfloor=k_a$.  A column meeting
$[a,a+2]$ in positive measure meets it.  The rows of $S_a$ and the columns of $B^{(0)}$ give
$|B_{3\times5}|=3\cdot5=15$.

(ii) $\Delta_{l,k}\subseteq B^{(j)}$ iff $|l|\le1+j$, $k\ge k_a-1-j$ and $k\le k_a+3+j$, i.e.\
iff $|l|-1\le j$, $k_a-1-k\le j$ and $k-k_a-3\le j$, i.e.\ iff $\delta(\Delta_{l,k})\le j$.
Since $\delta$ takes integer values, $\mathcal L_j=\{\delta=j\}$; these sets are disjoint
for different $j$, and $\Delta\not\subseteq B_{3\times5}=B^{(0)}$ iff $\delta(\Delta)\ge1$.
$B^{(j)}$ has $2(1+j)+1=3+2j$ rows and $(k_a+3+j)-(k_a-1-j)+1=5+2j$ columns, and
$B^{(j-1)}\subseteq B^{(j)}$, so
\[
 |\mathcal L_j|=(3+2j)(5+2j)-(1+2j)(3+2j)=(3+2j)\bigl[(5+2j)-(1+2j)\bigr]=4(3+2j)=8j+12 .
\]

(iii) $\delta(\Delta_{l,k})=1$ means: either $|l|=2$ and $k_a-2\le k\le k_a+4$ ($2\times7$
squares), or $|l|\le1$ and $k\in\{k_a-2,k_a+4\}$ ($3\times2$ squares); $14+6=20=8\cdot1+12$.

(iv) $S_a$ is the rectangle of Lemma~\ref{aux:notation-strip} with $l_1=l_2=0$, $k_1=k_a$,
$k_2=k_a+2$, and $\Delta\notin S_a$ since $\delta(\Delta)\ge1$.  Let $\Delta=\Delta_{l,k}\in
\mathcal L_j$.  If $|l|-1=j$, then $\rho_t=|l|=j+1\ge2$ and Lemma~\ref{aux:notation-strip}(i)
gives a parallel strip of width $j$.  If $k_a-1-k=j$, then $\rho_x=k_a-k=j+1$ and
Lemma~\ref{aux:notation-strip}(ii) gives a transverse strip of width $j$; if $k-k_a-3=j$, then
$\rho_x=k-(k_a+2)=j+1$, and again a transverse strip of width $j$.  At least one of the three
cases holds because $\delta(\Delta)=j$; if $|l|\le j$ the first fails, so $\Delta$ lies in a
side column.  Each strip separates $\Delta$ from $\bigcup S_a\supseteq\bigcup R$, so
$d(\Delta,R)\ge j$.  For a parallel strip $\{\alpha<x^0<\beta\}$ separating $\Delta$ from a
square $\Delta'\in R$ of row $0$, the computation in the proof of
Lemma~\ref{aux:notation-strip}(iv) gives $\beta-\alpha\le|l|-1$; for $|l|\le1$ this excludes
positive width.  The corners have $|l|=1+j$ and $k\in\{k_a-1-j,k_a+3+j\}$.

(v) $R$ is a non-empty subset of row $0$, and $|l-0|\le1$; apply
Lemma~\ref{aux:notation-strip}(iv).

(vi) By (ii) the sum over $\Delta\not\subseteq B_{3\times5}$ is the sum over $j\ge1$ of the
sums over $\mathcal L_j$, which have $8j+12$ terms each, all equal to $e^{-m_1w_\Delta}=x^j$;
this gives $\sum_\Delta e^{-m_1w_\Delta}=\sum_{j\ge1}(8j+12)x^j$.  By (iv), $d(\Delta,R)\ge
w_\Delta=j$ for $\Delta\in\mathcal L_j$, and $m_1>0$, so $e^{-m_1d(\Delta,R)}\le x^j$ and
$\Sigma_{3\times5}\le\overline\Sigma_{3\times5}$.  The closed form: for $0\le x<1$ and $N\ge1$,
\[
 (1-x)\sum_{j=1}^Nx^j=x-x^{N+1},\qquad
 (1-x)\sum_{j=1}^Njx^j=\sum_{j=1}^Nx^j-Nx^{N+1},
\]
and $x^{N+1}\to0$, $Nx^{N+1}\to0$ as $N\to\infty$; hence $\sum_{j\ge1}x^j=x/(1-x)$ and
$\sum_{j\ge1}jx^j=x/(1-x)^2$, and
\[
 \sum_{j\ge1}(8j+12)x^j=\frac{8x}{(1-x)^2}+\frac{12x}{1-x}
 =\frac{8x+12x-12x^2}{(1-x)^2}=\frac{4x(5-3x)}{(1-x)^2} .
\]
Test by hand: $4x(5-3x)(1-x)^{-2}=4x(5-3x)(1+2x+3x^2+\cdots)=20x+28x^2+\cdots$, and
$8\cdot1+12=20$, $8\cdot2+12=28$.
\end{proof}

\begin{auxrem}[comparison and check]\label{aux:notation-ringrem}
(a) The bound $(2j+3)(2j+5)$ of the plan (\texttt{aqft}) is $|B^{(j)}|$, the number of
squares of the box that contains $\mathcal L_j$; it is valid and cruder.
(b) \emph{Where the transverse case enters.}  The squares of rows $-1,0,1$ outside
$B_{3\times5}$ admit no parallel strip of positive width
(Lemma~\ref{aux:notation-rings}(v)).  The other side squares of $\mathcal L_j$, in rows
$2\le|l|\le j$, admit parallel strips, but only of width $\le|l|-1<j$; the bound
$d(\Delta,R)\ge j$ uses the transverse strip for them too.  It cannot be replaced by the
parallel width without losing the uniformity: for fixed $|l|\ge2$ the parallel width is the
same in every column, and the number of squares of row $l$ charged by $h$ is not bounded
uniformly in $h\in\CE$.  So the uniform bound $\overline\Sigma_{3\times5}$ of (vi), with the
strips of (iv), uses the transverse case of Theorem~1.1.7
for every square of $\mathcal L_j$ with $|l|\le j$, not only for rows $-1,0,1$; each such
use is the case A8(iv)-T of Assumption~\ref{A:gjs}, item (iv-T).
(c) \emph{Numerical check} (\texttt{ugjs/parts/cqft2\_ringcheck.py}; nothing is deduced from
it): for $j=1,\dots,12$ and several $a$, the script lists the squares with $\delta=j$,
confirms $8j+12$ of them, computes for each the axis-parallel width of
Lemma~\ref{aux:notation-strip} and confirms it is $\ge j$, and compares partial sums of
$\sum(8j+12)x^j$ with the closed form in exact rational arithmetic.  Run record: 3 November 2026,
\texttt{/usr/local/bin/python3} (Python 3.14.7, numpy 2.5.2, scipy 1.18.1), output
\texttt{ugjs/parts/cqft2\_ringcheck.output.txt}, ``ALL CHECKS PASSED''; the quadrature there uses
the relative tolerance $10^{-11}$, set in the script and not printed in the output; it also checks the kernel
bound of Lemma~\ref{aux:notation-kernel} against quadrature (relative tolerance $10^{-11}$) and
the inequality $(4n-1)!!\le4^n(2n)!$ of \S\ref{app:D-constants} for $n\le40$.
\end{auxrem}
